# Fylz Phase 0–1 progress log

Baseline: `a04323f`. Branch: `claude/fylz-fotoz-complete-y60pfw` (see note below).

**Branch note:** section 2 of `FYLZ_CLAUDE_CODE_INSTRUCTIONS.md` specifies branching as
`agent/fylz-p0-p1` from `main`. This run's harness assigns a fixed shared branch,
`claude/fylz-fotoz-complete-y60pfw`, across this repo and four others; a different branch name
would leave the work unpushable through the harness's PR flow. The branch already exists at the
`main`/baseline commit, so this is a naming substitution only — every other instruction (one
commit per task, draft PR, never merge to main, etc.) is followed as written.

**Build environment note:** JDK 17 is not installed in this session's container; JDK 21 is used
instead (confirmed compatible — the full Gradle configuration phase, submodule composite build,
and a `:app:tasks` run all succeed under it). Everything else in section 0's preflight matches:
Gradle wrapper 8.14.3, Android SDK platform 36 + build-tools 36.0.0 installed, x86_64 host so no
`aapt2` override is needed.

**Maven Central rate limiting:** this sandbox's shared egress IP is rate-limited (HTTP 429) by
`repo.maven.apache.org`, intermittently and on a large fraction of requests, independent of
anything in this repo. Backoff retries get a clean dependency resolution through eventually; once
the local Gradle/Maven caches are warm for a given dependency set, subsequent runs are fast and
unaffected. Noted per-task below only where it actually delayed verification.

## Tasks

| P0.0 | done, verified | (this commit for the fixes below; see prior commit for the original harness) | `storage/FylzFilesDocumentsProvider.kt` (test seam only); `storage/FylzDocumentsProviderTestBase.kt`, `storage/FaultyDocumentsProvider.kt`, `storage/FaultyDocumentsProviderTest.kt`, `storage/FylzFilesDocumentsProviderSmokeTest.kt`, new `storage/ProviderTestSupport.kt` | Robolectric smoke tests (list/create/read/write through the hosted provider), `FaultyDocumentsProvider` self-tests (refuse rename/delete/create, truncated write), tree-fixture build/diff tests | Robolectric, `testDebugUnitTest` green (33/33 in `storage`+`operations`, stable across 3 repeat runs) | Getting P0.0's harness to actually pass (once P0.1 exercised it through real `ContentResolver` routing, not just direct method calls) surfaced three real bugs in the harness itself, all fixed: (1) `Robolectric.setupContentProvider(Class, authority)` builds a synthetic `ProviderInfo` that leaves `exported`/`grantUriPermissions`/the `MANAGE_DOCUMENTS` permissions unset, which the real `DocumentsProvider.attachInfo()` requires — fixed by building a `ProviderInfo` by hand (`ProviderTestSupport.testProviderInfo`) matching the app's real manifest entry, and explicitly registering with `ShadowContentResolver` for tests that route through `Context.contentResolver` rather than calling provider methods directly. (2) `FaultyDocumentsProvider.throwAfterBytes`'s pipe-based "broken pipe on the caller's write" design doesn't work under Robolectric — confirmed empirically that neither a plain pipe's OS backpressure nor a reliable pipe's `closeWithError()` propagates to the writer side under this Robolectric version's `ParcelFileDescriptor` shadow. Redesigned around what IS reliable: the real target file ends up truncated at exactly the configured limit, verified by joining the background copy thread and checking file length, documented as a known Robolectric limitation in `throwAfterBytes`'s KDoc for whoever relies on this double next (P0.6). |
| P0.1 | done | (this commit) | New `operations/DocNode.kt`; rewrote `operations/FileOperationService.kt` on it; new `operations/FileOperationServiceTest.kt` | 3-level/20-file copy (byte-identical via `diffTrees`), the same as a move (source gone, destination identical), move with delete refused → `MOVE_SOURCE_DELETE_PENDING` → `finishMoveCleanup` completes without re-copying | Robolectric, `testDebugUnitTest` green, stable across 3 repeat runs | `DocNode` (A1) replaces `DocumentFile` in `FileOperationService`: `fromSingleUri(...).listFiles()` always threw (the actual defect 1 root cause — confirmed still present before the fix), so every folder copy/move failed. Verification now happens per-file inside the copy recursion (`verifyFile`, called for every leaf, not just a single top-level check that was meaningless for directories) — a move's source is only deleted after every nested item has verified clean. `DocNode` uses the `(Uri, String[], Bundle?, CancellationSignal?)` query overload throughout, not the classic `(selection, selectionArgs, sortOrder)` one: `DocumentsProvider`'s base class throws `UnsupportedOperationException("Pre-Android-O query format not supported.")` from the classic overload, confirmed under this Robolectric-hosted real provider for both a single-document and a children query. `DocNode.delete()` catches and reports `false` on failure rather than letting an exception propagate, matching `DocumentsContract.deleteDocument`'s documented boolean contract and what every caller (chiefly the `MOVE_SOURCE_DELETE_PENDING` fallback) already assumed. |
| P0.2 | done, verified | (this commit) | Rewrote `operations/RecycleBinService.kt` on `DocNode`; extended `DocNode.kt` (`findChild`, `canWrite`); `operations/RecycleBinPolicy.kt` and `operations/RecycleBinStore.kt` gained `RecycleRecord.containerUri`; `ui/FylzV1App.kt`'s `recycleSelection()` simplified onto `recycleRootFor`; fixed `storage/FylzFilesDocumentsProvider.kt`'s `sanitizeDisplayName`; fixed `storage/FaultyDocumentsProvider.kt`'s rename fault injection; fixed `storage/testing/TreeFixtures.kt`'s `diffTrees`; new `operations/RecycleBinServiceTest.kt` | Recycle-and-restore a folder tree byte-identical; restore into a name conflict under Skip/Keep-both/Replace; a refused rename on the first (destructive) step keeps the record and touches nothing; a refused rename on the second (Replace) step rolls the first back | Robolectric, `testDebugUnitTest` green (184/184 across the full suite), `lintDebug` clean | Fixed defect 1 for real: every `<uuid>` recycle-transaction folder and the replaced-item "aside" file is now tracked via `RecycleRecord.containerUri` / `DocNode`-based lookups, never `DocumentFile.fromSingleUri(...).parentFile` (always null). Replace never deletes before the replacement is confirmed (rename-aside → rename-into-place → recycle-or-delete-aside → rollback-on-failure). Writing this test surfaced a real, pre-existing production bug in `FylzFilesDocumentsProvider.sanitizeDisplayName`: it unconditionally stripped every leading `.` from a requested name (`trimStart('.')`), so a request for `.fylz-trash` actually created a directory named `fylz-trash` — silently defeating `RecycleBinService`'s own by-name lookup of its recycle root (`findRecycleRoot`'s `it.name == RECYCLE_DIRECTORY` check), `RecursiveSearchEngine`'s skip-the-trash-folder check, and `FylzV1App`'s hide-the-trash-folder browse filter, all three of which compare against the literal `.fylz-trash` string. Fixed by only rejecting `.`/`..` as reserved names instead of stripping every leading dot — this app grants full filesystem access, so a user-legitimate hidden dotfile is not itself a name to refuse. Also fixed `FaultyDocumentsProvider`'s rename fault injection, which refused every call from the configured Nth call *onward*; the Replace-rollback path needs exactly one rename to fail and the very next one (the rollback) to succeed, so it now refuses only the exact Nth call (renamed `refuseRenameFromCall` → `refuseRenameAtCall` for clarity). Also extended the test-only `diffTrees` helper to compare two plain files directly when given non-directory paths, not just directory trees (needed to assert a rolled-back single-file replace target). |
| P0.3 | done, verified | (this commit) | `storage/FylzFilesDocumentsProvider.kt`'s `sanitizeDisplayName` refined further; `operations/RecycleBinService.kt` gained `legacyRecycleFolders`/`legacyRecycleFolderNames`/`tidyLegacyRecycleFolder`; `search/RecursiveSearchEngine.kt`'s `search()` gained an `excludedDirectoryNames` parameter; `ui/FylzV1App.kt` computes and applies `legacyBinNames` (browse filter, search exclusion, and a new "Tidy legacy recycle folders" action in `RecycleBinDialog`); new `storage/FylzFilesDocumentsProviderDotfileTest.kt`, new `operations/RecycleBinLegacyFolderTest.kt`, one added case in `operations/RecycleBinServiceTest.kt` | Dot-prefixed names (`.gitignore`, `.nomedia`, a `.git/` folder) survive creation, copy and move; blank/`.`/`..`/`/`-containing/NUL names are rejected, other control characters still replaced; recycling twice reuses the same `.fylz-trash`; a `fylz-trash`-named folder is treated as legacy only when an existing `RecycleRecord` points inside it, never on name alone; tidying moves recorded items into the real bin one at a time and removes the legacy folder only once it is verified empty, leaving untracked content in place | Robolectric, `testDebugUnitTest` green (195/195 across the full suite), `lintDebug` clean | The dot-stripping fix itself (defect 3's core bug) already landed in the P0.2 commit, since P0.2's own tests needed a working `.fylz-trash` to pass; this task completes the rest of defect 3's scope. Tightened the reject list to match spec exactly: a name containing `/` or a NUL byte is now rejected (`IllegalArgumentException`) rather than silently replaced with `_`, so a caller finds out immediately rather than getting a mangled name; other control characters are still replaced, unchanged. Legacy-bin detection uses `DocumentsContract.isChildDocument(resolver, candidateUri, recordUri)` to decide whether a `fylz-trash`/`fylz-trash (n)` folder is actually referenced by a live `RecycleRecord` — confirmed empirically that this call routes correctly through Robolectric's provider `call()` dispatch, the same mechanism already proven for `createDocument`/`renameDocument`/`deleteDocument` in P0.0–P0.2, so no workaround was needed. Tidying is entirely manual (no automatic migration, per contract §2): a legacy folder is deleted only once every item a record still points to has been copied, verified and recorded at its new location, and only if nothing untracked is left behind — mirrors the Replace rollback's never-delete-before-confirmed discipline from P0.2. |
| P0.4 | done, verified | (this commit) | Rewrote `operations/FileTools.kt`'s `executeBatchRename` on `DocNode` with an explicit `parentUri` parameter (A2); `findDuplicates` also moved off `DocumentFile` onto `DocNode` (A1 names `FileTools` explicitly); `ui/FylzV1App.kt`'s `BatchRenameDialog` gained inline prefix validation and an old→new preview list, and its confirm handler now runs planning and execution inside the same `runCatching`; new `operations/FileToolsTest.kt` | 10-file prefix rename with content verified byte-for-byte; a two-item swap cycle (`a`→`b`, `b`→`a`) with no spurious self-collision; a target colliding with an untouched file is refused before anything is renamed; a provider that refuses the 6th rename rolls the whole batch back to its original names and content | Robolectric, `testDebugUnitTest` green (199/199 across the full suite), `lintDebug` clean | `fromSingleUri(...).parentFile` was null, so every batch rename failed outright; `executeBatchRename(parentUri, plans)` now resolves the parent once via `DocNode.load` and renames every item through `DocNode.rename`, tracking each item's current node in a mutable `RenameStep` (`DocNode` is immutable, so a step's uri changes across the stage→finalize→(rollback) sequence and must be re-read each time). `planBatchRename` also crashed the whole app outside `runCatching` for a prefix producing an illegal name (e.g. containing `/`); the dialog now calls it defensively for a live preview on every keystroke and the confirm handler wraps both planning and execution in one `runCatching`. Writing the swap-cycle and 10-file tests surfaced a URI-construction trap worth recording: `FylzFilesDocumentsProvider.documentUri(rootId, path)` builds a *self-anchored* uri (its own tree segment equals its own document id) — fine for a query/read/delete, but `DocumentsProvider.enforceTree` rejects it the moment a rename changes the document id out from under a tree segment still pointing at the old path. Production is never exposed to this: `DocumentRepository.listChildren` always anchors `FileEntry.uri` to the tab's one stable `treeUri`, never to a per-item path. Fixing the test's uri construction (not production code, which was never exposed to this) also resolved a second symptom of the same root cause: the swap-cycle test's `externalCollision` check compared a self-anchored source uri string against tree-anchored sibling uri strings for the same document and never found them equal, so it spuriously refused the swap as a collision with itself. |
| P0.5 | done, verified | (this commit) | New `operations/OperationRunner.kt` (`RunningOperation`, `OperationProgress`); `FylzApplication.kt` gained an app-scoped `operationScope`/`operationRunner`; `operations/OperationModels.kt`'s `FileOperationType` gained `PDF`; `ui/FylzV1App.kt`'s copy/move/extract (`destinationPicker`), `recycleSelection()` and the PDF merge/export launch (`pdfOutputCreator`) now run through `operationRunner.run(...)` instead of `rememberCoroutineScope()`, and `FileBrowser` gained a real per-operation progress row (determinate when bytes or item counts are known, indeterminate otherwise) with a Cancel button, shown above the file list rather than swapping it out; `AndroidManifest.xml`'s `MainActivity` gained the interim `android:configChanges`; new `operations/OperationRunnerTest.kt` | A runner job keeps running after the observing (UI) scope is cancelled; `cancel(id)` stops the tracked job and clears it from `operations`; progress reported mid-job updates the tracked `RunningOperation` | Robolectric + plain JVM, `testDebugUnitTest` green (202/202 across the full suite), `lintDebug` clean, `assembleDebug` succeeds | Implements A3: `OperationRunner.run` launches on `FylzApplication.operationScope` (a `SupervisorJob() + Dispatchers.Default` that outlives every Activity/composable) via `scope.async {}` and returns the `Deferred`; the UI's own `scope.launch { ... .await() ... }` only awaits the result to update its own state on completion, so tearing down the composable (rotation, folding, resizing) stops the UI from *seeing* the result without touching the operation itself. This also fixes "the journal records `USER_CANCELLED` only when the user presses Cancel" as a side effect rather than a separate mechanism: since nothing but an explicit `OperationRunner.cancel(id)` call (wired only to the new Cancel button) can ever cancel a job's `Deferred` now, every `CancellationException` a service's journal sees really is user-initiated -- previously, composable teardown cancelled `rememberCoroutineScope()` and was indistinguishable from the user pressing Cancel. Copy/move/extract/recycle/PDF are the five operations named in the task's own file list; batch rename, find-duplicates, archive-create and the recycle bin's restore/delete/tidy actions still run on the UI's own scope, unchanged, since they're out of this task's named scope. `ArchiveService.extractZip` has no progress callback of its own today, so its row is indeterminate; adding one is future work, not named here. |

`operations/FileOperationService.kt` rewrote staging: every copy now writes under a new top-level `stagingName`/`isStagingName`/`STAGING_NAME_PREFIX` (`.fylz-part-<operationId>-<itemIndex>-<name>`, ≤255 UTF-8 bytes) instead of `TargetPlan`'s old per-policy staging (removed, along with `uniqueStagingName`), and `finalizeTarget` renames staged→final directly when there's no conflict; new `operations/OperationRunner.recover(journal, resolver)` (companion function); `FylzApplication.kt` constructs the journal first and calls `recover` on the operation scope at app start; `ui/FylzV1App.kt`'s folder listing and `search/RecursiveSearchEngine.kt`'s traversal both hide `isStagingName` entries; `ui/components/OperationHistoryDialog.kt` presents `INTERRUPTED`; new `operations/StagingNameTest.kt`, `operations/OperationRunnerRecoverTest.kt`, one added case in `operations/FileOperationServiceTest.kt` | Cancelling mid-copy (a real coroutine cancellation, not a crash) leaves nothing at all in the destination, staging document included; `recover()` deletes exactly the one recorded staging document and marks the item `INTERRUPTED`, leaving unrelated files and journal records untouched; `recover()` is a no-op with nothing to recover; a retry after recovery, replayed through `OperationRetryPolicy.plan()` exactly as `FylzAppShell`'s own retry button would, succeeds and reproduces the file; the staging-name helper carries the operation id/item index/name, neutralizes `/`, stays within 255 UTF-8 bytes without ever splitting a multi-byte character, and round-trips through `isStagingName` | Robolectric + plain JVM, `testDebugUnitTest` green (211/211 across the full suite), `lintDebug` clean, `assembleDebug` succeeds | Every copy previously wrote under its *final* name unless the conflict policy was Replace (`resolveTargetPlan`'s `stagingName == requestedName` for the plain-create and Keep-both cases), so a process death mid-write left a half-written file exactly where the user would see it as "done." Since a top-level directory copy also created its node directly under the final name before recursing into children, a folder copy had the identical defect; fixed by staging directories the same way as files (recursion inside stays unstaged, since it already lives inside a hidden `.fylz-part-*` parent — nested children don't need their own staging name). `OperationJournal`'s own construction-time recovery (pre-existing, P0.5 research) already relabels a dead process's `RUNNING`/`PREFLIGHT`/`PAUSED` items to `NEEDS_ATTENTION`/`"PROCESS_INTERRUPTED"`; `OperationRunner.recover` is a deliberate second, narrower pass layered on top of that (not a replacement for it) that only touches copy/move items carrying that exact error code, so it never second-guesses an unrelated `NEEDS_ATTENTION` case like `MOVE_SOURCE_DELETE_PENDING`, which keeps its own recovery path (`finishMoveCleanup`). Ordering matters: `recover` assumes the journal's own recovery already ran, so `FylzApplication.onCreate()` constructs the journal (a synchronous side effect of its `init` block) before launching `recover` on the app scope — every other `OperationJournal` constructed later in the same process sees the same static per-process session guard and skips re-running it. |
| P0.7 | done, verified | (this commit) | Rewrote `data/DocumentRepository.kt`'s `readText`/`writeText`: `TextContent` (now top-level) gained `encodingOk`/`hasBom`/`lineEnding`/`editable`, a new top-level `LineEnding` enum and `SaveResult` sealed interface (`Success`/`Failed(message)`), and a `TEXT_EDIT_LIMIT_BYTES` constant; `ui/components/PreviewPane.kt` gained a `textEncodingOk` parameter and now refuses to show the editor (with a visible reason, in place of the Edit button) when the read was truncated or not valid UTF-8; `ui/FylzV1App.kt` gained `previewEncodingOk`/`previewHasBom` state and a single `saveEditorText()` used by both the docked and floating `PreviewPane` call sites; `storage/FaultyDocumentsProvider.kt` gained a test-only `reportedSizeOverride` fault-injection hook; new `data/DocumentRepositoryTextTest.kt` | Plain ASCII text decodes cleanly and stays editable; a file over the 512 KiB edit limit reports `truncated` and `editable = false`, with the read stopped at exactly the byte cap; a Latin-1 byte sequence fails strict UTF-8 decoding (`encodingOk = false`, not editable) but still returns a best-effort lenient decode for preview; a CRLF file carrying a BOM round-trips byte-identical through read→edit→save (BOM stripped from the editor value, re-added on save); a simulated post-write size mismatch restores the pre-write cached copy and reports `SaveResult.Failed` rather than leaving the file half-changed | Robolectric, `testDebugUnitTest` green (216/216 across the full suite), `lintDebug` clean, `assembleDebug` succeeds | The editor previously decoded everything as lenient UTF-8 (any Latin-1/UTF-16 file silently became mojibake), capped the read at 512K *characters* with no way for the caller to know a save would be lossy, and `writeText` had no post-write verification at all — a truncated provider write or an `openOutputStream("wt")`→`"w"` fallback landing on a different actual size than intended went completely unnoticed. `readText` now reads at most `TEXT_EDIT_LIMIT_BYTES` (512 KiB) raw bytes, detects and strips a leading UTF-8 BOM, trims a byte-capped read's possibly-incomplete trailing multi-byte sequence before decoding (so truncation is never misreported as malformed input), and decodes strictly (`CodingErrorAction.REPORT`) with a lenient fallback (`REPLACE`) kept only for still showing *something* in preview. `TextContent.editable` (`!truncated && encodingOk`) is the single gate `PreviewPane` uses to refuse editing with a reason, collapsing the brief's two size/truncation conditions into one, since the byte cap already governs both. `writeText` now takes `hasBom` (re-adds the BOM bytes on save so a file that had one stays byte-identical), keeps a pre-write copy in `context.cacheDir` for the duration of the save — a new, always-on, transient mechanism, deliberately not `FileHistoryStore`, which is opt-in, permanently retained, and size-capped, so unsuitable as this brief's safety net — and re-queries the provider's reported size after writing; a mismatch restores the cached copy and reports `SaveResult.Failed` with a message telling the user whether the restore itself succeeded. Every path returns a `SaveResult` rather than throwing (a stray `CancellationException` is re-thrown, never swallowed, so real coroutine cancellation still works). Writing `DocumentRepositoryTextTest` surfaced a second instance of the same `DocumentsProvider` quirk P0.1 already documented for `DocNode`: the classic `(Uri, String[], String, String[], String)` query overload throws `"Pre-Android-O query format not supported."` when called directly under Robolectric instead of going through the real framework's Bundle-bridging; `writeText`'s new `querySize()` helper uses the same `(Uri, String[], Bundle?, CancellationSignal?)` overload `DocNode` already established as the working pattern. The size-mismatch test needed a deterministic way to make a provider report a wrong post-write size without racing `FaultyDocumentsProvider`'s existing async `throwAfterBytes` truncating pipe (whose own KDoc already warns its effect on the underlying file is only visible after joining a background thread) — added `reportedSizeOverride`, scoped narrowly to a `COLUMN_SIZE`-only query, as a second, independent, synchronous fault-injection hook alongside it. |
| P0.8 | done, verified | (this commit) | `operations/RecycleBinStore.kt` gained a `records: StateFlow<List<RecycleRecord>>` refreshed after every `put`/`remove`; `operations/RecycleBinService.kt` exposes it (`records` property, alongside the unchanged one-shot `records()`) and gained `emptyBin(confirmed: Boolean): Int`; `ui/components/PermanentDeleteConfirmationDialog.kt` regeneralized from a single `displayName` to `itemCount`/`totalBytes`/`itemName?`, with its copy pulled into a testable top-level `permanentDeleteMessage` plus a `totalKnownBytes` aggregator; `ui/FylzV1App.kt` collects `recycleBin.records` instead of a one-shot call, routes every permanent delete (single item and a new "Empty Recycle Bin" button in `RecycleBinDialog`) through the confirmation dialog via a new `PermanentDeleteRequest`, and drops the no-op `recycleDialog = false; recycleDialog = true` refresh hack; three new/updated tests in `operations/RecycleBinServiceTest.kt`, new `ui/components/PermanentDeleteConfirmationDialogTest.kt` | The records flow reflects a recycle and a permanent delete with no manual re-read; emptying the bin deletes every record, reports how many succeeded, and leaves the bin folder itself clean; emptying without confirmation is refused (`IllegalArgumentException`) and deletes nothing; the confirmation copy is exactly right for a single named item (with and without a known size), for emptying a plural and a singular bin, and for a sub-KiB size; `totalKnownBytes` sums known sizes, ignoring unknown ones, and is null only when none are known | Robolectric + plain JVM, `testDebugUnitTest` green (227/227 across the full suite), `lintDebug` clean, `assembleDebug` succeeds | `permanentlyDelete(id, confirmed = true)` was called directly from the Recycle Bin dialog's "Delete permanently" button with no prompt at all -- the `confirmed` parameter existed only to satisfy `RecycleBinPolicy.allowPermanentDelete`'s signature, never actually gated by a user action. It's now hardcoded `true` only inside the one place that follows an actual confirmed dialog (`FylzV1App`'s two `onConfirmed` closures); `PermanentDeleteConfirmationDialog` requires typing `DELETE PERMANENTLY` before either closure runs, unchanged from its pre-existing (previously unused) high-friction design. Kept `RecycleBinStore.list()`/`find()` reading fresh from `SharedPreferences` on every call, unchanged: `FileOperationService` and `DuplicateCleanupService` each construct their own `RecycleBinService`/`RecycleBinStore` (confirmed by grep, not assumed), so caching `list()` itself off the new in-memory `StateFlow` would have let a permanent-delete or recycle made through one of *those* instances go unseen by the UI's own store instance until its next own mutation overwrote it with stale data -- a real data-loss risk. The new `records` `StateFlow` only mirrors *this instance's own* `put`/`remove` calls (documented as such), which is exactly what the UI's single, `remember`-scoped `RecycleBinService` needs and is no less correct than the one-shot read it replaces. `emptyBin` reuses `permanentlyDelete` per record rather than duplicating its journal/store bookkeeping, and continues past one record's failure so a single bad item can't block emptying the rest. The dialog's `displayName: String` parameter is gone; a single delete now passes `itemCount = 1` with the record's own name in the new nullable `itemName`, preserving the original "deletes "name"" wording, while Empty Recycle Bin passes only a count/total (no single name makes sense there) -- `permanentDeleteMessage` unifies both into one tested function instead of the dialog needing two message-building code paths. |

extensions the registry already treated as text but this class used to miss (`lua`, `cfg`, `srt`, `vtt`, `tex`, `bib`) now classify as `EntryKind.TEXT`; a real three-packet MPEG-TS header (sync byte `0x47` at offsets 0/188/376) is recognized, a TypeScript source header is not, a header no longer than one packet is never confirmed, a coincidental leading sync byte without the offset-188 repeat is rejected, and two confirmed syncs are enough when a third packet isn't available | Robolectric + plain JVM, `testDebugUnitTest` green (233/233 across the full suite), `lintDebug` clean, `assembleDebug` succeeds | Confirmed via a research subagent sweep of the whole `app/src/main/java` tree before touching routing: `FileFormatRegistry.describe(...)` has exactly one call site (`PreviewPane.kt`), so no other screen needed updating for the `cad2d`→`images` move; `.depth`/`.rendered` are read nowhere (dead fields today), `.notes` is shown as a warning banner only inside `UniversalInspectorPreview` (which is only reached via `PreviewPane`'s own `else` fallback or `RichImagePreview`'s new error path) -- so marking formats `INSPECTED` only surfaces visibly once `RichImagePreview` actually falls back to it on a decode error, which this task also wires up; `svg`'s `rendererId` was already `"image"` before this change (set inside the old CAD branch), so the `rendererId == "image"` routing addition is defense in depth, not what actually fixes SVG here (removing it from `cad2d` is). `resolvePreviewKind`'s `.ts` disambiguation defaults to TEXT and only defers to `entry.kind` when the header confirms MPEG-TS -- the opposite of what "returns TEXT unless the header is MPEG-TS" first reads as intuitively backwards for: the actual defect is TypeScript *source* files getting misclassified as video (not the reverse), because `FileType.classify`'s MIME-before-extension ordering trusts a provider's naive extension-based MIME guess (`video/mp2t` for literally any `.ts` file) before ever consulting the extension-based text set -- sniffing the real bytes is the only way to tell the two apart, and TypeScript source is the common case a general file manager sees under this extension, hence the TEXT-favoring default. `resolvePreviewKind` is deliberately *not* folded into `FileType.classify` itself (which stays extension/MIME-only, unchanged for this ambiguity): sniffing 512 bytes per row would be far too expensive for a folder listing, so it's used only at the two actual preview-decision points named in the brief. The previewMode reopen fix lives inside `BoxWithConstraints` (not at the top of the composable) because `wide` is only known there; keying the `LaunchedEffect` on `focusedEntry?.uri` alone (not `wide`) means a width change alone (rotation) never forces a re-float, only an actual new selection does. |
| P0.10 | done, verified | (this commit) | `ui/FylzV1App.kt`: `canExtract` gated on a new `isZipFamilyArchive` (zip/zipx/jar/apk/cbz only, since `ArchiveService.extractZip` is a ZIP reader); `onExtract` now inspects the archive first and prompts for a password via a new `PasswordField` (masked, show/hide toggle) when `ArchiveInspection.encrypted`, reused for the pre-existing (already-masked-nowhere) AI API key and WebDAV password fields too; two `\${…}` escaped-interpolation bugs fixed in the PDF export/merge output names, pulled into testable `pdfPagesFileName`/`pdfMergedFileName`; PDF merge/export `sources` now built through a new `orderedBySelection` (selection order) instead of folder-listing order; new `browse/OpenTabsStore.kt` records only URIs opened via `openTabAt`, and tab restoration on launch reads from it (filtered to grants the OS still reports) instead of every persisted `ContentResolver` grant. `README.md`: removed/marked-planned the details view, duplicate action and selectable-conflict-policy claims the running app doesn't meet, and corrected the Gradle version to 8.14.3. New `ui/FylzV1AppLogicTest.kt`, new `browse/OpenTabsStoreTest.kt` | ZIP-family extensions are recognized as extractable, everything else isn't; PDF output names actually interpolate the timestamp instead of the literal `${…}` text; PDF sources preserve selection order regardless of folder-listing order, and skip a selected uri no longer present in the listing rather than crashing; a fresh `OpenTabsStore` has nothing to restore; recorded trees come back in the order they were opened; recording the same tree twice keeps its original position | Robolectric + plain JVM, `testDebugUnitTest` green (240/240 across the full suite), `lintDebug` clean, `assembleDebug` succeeds | Verified each README claim against the running app rather than trusting the brief's list at face value, since P0.5 (this session) already made real cancellation true -- confirmed via `ViewMode.DETAILS`/`onDuplicate`/`AccentPreset` picker searches (zero non-declaration hits for all three) that details view, duplicate and accent-preset *selection* are genuinely still unimplemented, so only those plus the conflict-policy-picker claim were softened; "optional dynamic colour" was left alone since it actually runs (`dynamicColor = true`, applied via `dynamicDarkColorScheme`/`dynamicLightColorScheme` on API 31+), just not user-toggleable, which the brief didn't name. `orderedBySelection` relies on `selectedUris`'s *runtime* iteration order already being insertion order (the Kotlin stdlib's `Set<T>.plus`/`.minus` extensions are documented to build a `LinkedHashSet`), rather than changing `selectedUris`'s declared type or its dozen mutation call sites across the file -- confirmed by reading every one of them, all already going through `+`/`-`/`.toSet()` on a list, never anything that would silently reorder. This only reorders the *derivation* used for the PDF dialog specifically; `selectedEntries` (used by copy/move/recycle/rename/tags/share/batch rename) is untouched, since folder order is the right, and already correct, order for all of those. `OpenTabsStore` only records through `openTabAt` -- confirmed via a whole-file grep that `destinationPicker` (copy/move/extract) and every other `persistTreePermission`/`takePersistableUriPermission` call site outside `FylzV1App.kt` (backup, index, archive tools, backup import) never calls it -- so none of those destinations become a restored tab, exactly the defect's fix. No UI test exists for the Extract password prompt or the masked-field toggle in this repo (no Compose UI test harness, consistent with every prior UI-only change this session); verified by `assembleDebug` and code review instead, noted honestly rather than assumed passing. |
| P0.11 | done, verified | (this commit) | `network/SftpProvider.kt`: removed `PinnedHostKeyVerifier` (hand-rolled, hashed `key.encoded` -- X.509/DER); new `sha256Fingerprint(key)` hashes the SSH wire-format blob (`Buffer.PlainBuffer().putPublicKey(key).compactData`) instead, matching `ssh-keygen -lf`; new `hostKeyVerifierFor(fingerprint)` normalizes and delegates to sshj's own `FingerprintVerifier.getInstance` rather than hand-rolled comparison; new `isPlausibleHostKeyFingerprint` (relaxed to also accept legacy MD5 colon-hex, not just SHA-256 base64) backs `SftpProviderConfig`'s `init` check; new `CapturingHostKeyVerifier` (always rejects, remembers the presented key) and `SftpProvider.probeHostKeyFingerprint(host, port)` implement trust-on-first-use steps 1-2 (no credentials needed, since host-key exchange precedes authentication). Deleted unused, identically-bugged `network/SftpService.kt`. `ui/RemoteConnectionsDialog.kt`'s SFTP form gained a "Detect fingerprint" button (calls `probeHostKeyFingerprint`) and a confirmation dialog that only fills the fingerprint field in after the user explicitly confirms it (TOFU step 3 -- probing alone never pins anything). New `network/SftpHostKeyFingerprintTest.kt`, backed by two real key pairs generated with `ssh-keygen` (`app/src/test/resources/sftp/{ed25519,rsa}_test.pub`) | `sha256Fingerprint` matches real `ssh-keygen -lf` output for both a real ed25519 and a real RSA key; a verifier pinned to the `SHA256:` form, a bare unprefixed form, and the legacy MD5 form all accept their matching key and reject the other fixture's key; `isPlausibleHostKeyFingerprint` accepts every real shape (prefixed/bare SHA-256, prefixed/bare MD5) and rejects blank/garbage input; `SftpProviderConfig` accepts a legacy MD5 fingerprint, not just SHA-256; `CapturingHostKeyVerifier` remembers the presented key but never returns `true` | Robolectric + plain JVM, `testDebugUnitTest` green (248/248 across the full suite), `lintDebug` clean, `assembleDebug` succeeds | The defect was exactly as described: `MessageDigest.getInstance("SHA-256").digest(key.encoded)` hashes `PublicKey.getEncoded()`'s X.509 SubjectPublicKeyInfo DER encoding, a completely different byte sequence from the SSH wire-format blob every real OpenSSH fingerprint (`ssh-keygen -lf`, `ssh-keyscan`) is computed from -- host-key pinning was silently unenforceable: no fingerprint a user could ever obtain and paste in would match, for any server, ever. Confirmed the fix against ground truth rather than re-deriving the SSH wire format by hand: installed `openssh-client` in this sandbox, generated real ed25519 and RSA key pairs, and captured `ssh-keygen -lf`'s actual SHA-256 and MD5 output as the tests' expected values -- both matched on the first run. Used `javap -c` to decompile sshj 0.40.0's actual `FingerprintVerifier`/`SecurityUtils` bytecode (not memory or an assumed API) to confirm `getInstance`'s exact accepted formats (`SHA1:`/`SHA256:`/`MD5:`-prefixed, or bare colon-hex MD5 -- notably *not* bare unprefixed SHA-256, which `SftpProviderConfig` stores, hence `hostKeyVerifierFor`'s normalization step) before writing any production code against it. Chose *not* to weaken `SftpProviderConfig`'s existing requirement that every real connection have a pinned fingerprint (TOFU only ever populates the field via explicit user confirmation in the UI, never bypasses the requirement itself) -- `probeHostKeyFingerprint` needs no `SftpProviderConfig` at all, since it only runs the SSH transport's key exchange, never authentication. **Device check still needed** (sandboxed container, no real SFTP server reachable): connect to a real server, and if key exchange fails with algorithm errors, register the bundled BouncyCastle at startup (`Security.removeProvider("BC"); Security.insertProviderAt(BouncyCastleProvider(), 1)`) per the brief -- carried into `docs/agent/DEVICE_CHECKS.md` at Phase 0 exit. |

New `MainActivityTest.kt`, new `data/DocumentRepositoryExternalEntryTest.kt` | `viewUriFrom` extracts an `ACTION_VIEW` intent's data, returns null for a `MAIN`/launcher intent and for a null intent; `resolveExternalEntry` resolves the real name/size/kind of a document through generic `OpenableColumns`, and falls back to the uri's last path segment when nothing answers the query (an authority with no registered provider at all, matching how a live `ContentResolver` actually behaves for one) | Robolectric + plain JVM, `testDebugUnitTest` green (253/253 across the full suite), `lintDebug` clean, `assembleDebug` succeeds (including a full manifest merge -- confirmed manifest edits alone don't silently break the build, since a first attempt didn't: XML comments can't contain `--`, which `ManifestMerger2` rejected with a parse error at the exact line, fixed by rewording instead of using an em-dash-style separator) | The single-document dialog deliberately reuses `PreviewPane` wholesale rather than building a second preview surface: every format-specific renderer, the P0.7 edit-safety gating, and P0.9's `.ts` sniffing all apply identically to a file opened from outside, for free, with zero duplicated logic -- only Share and Save-a-copy-to (which browsing's own selection action bar normally supplies) needed adding. Keying the resolving `LaunchedEffect` on `viewUri` itself (not `Unit`) matters: a *second* "Open with Fylz" while Fylz is already running is delivered via `onNewIntent` under `singleTask`, updating the very same `mutableStateOf` `MainActivity` already exposes to Compose, so the effect re-fires for the new uri without needing an explicit "consumed" reset -- confirmed by reasoning through Compose's own recomposition-on-state-read contract rather than assumed. **Device checks still needed** (this sandboxed container has no real device/launcher): the three launcher shortcuts still open now that their targets are `exported="false"`, and the full "Open with Fylz" flow (another app's share sheet or file browser choosing Fylz, both first-launch via `onCreate` and while Fylz is already running via `onNewIntent`) -- both carried into `docs/agent/DEVICE_CHECKS.md` at Phase 0 exit, per the brief's own list. |
| P0.13 | done, verified | (this commit) | New `pdf/OcrEngine.kt`: `OcrScript` (moved here from `SearchablePdfService.kt`), `OcrEngine` (`suspend fun recognize(bitmap): Text`, `AutoCloseable`), `OcrEngineFactory` (`fun interface`), `MlKitOcrEngine` (wraps `TextRecognition.getClient(...)` for both scripts; its companion object *is* the default `OcrEngineFactory`), and the shared `internal suspend fun <T> Task<T>.await()` extension (previously duplicated: a private copy in `SearchablePdfService.kt`, a blocking `Tasks.await(...)` call in `PdfToolService.kt`). `pdf/SearchablePdfService.kt`: removed its local `OcrScript` enum, its private `recognizer(script)` method and its private `Task<T>.await()`; gained an `ocrEngineFactory: OcrEngineFactory = MlKitOcrEngine.Companion` constructor parameter, calls `recognizer.recognize(bitmap)` instead of building/driving a `TextRecognizer` itself. `pdf/PdfToolService.kt`: same seam (`ocrEngineFactory` constructor parameter, same default), always requests `OcrScript.LATIN` as it always implicitly did (no public API change), replaced the blocking `Tasks.await(recognizer.process(...))` with the suspend `recognizer.recognize(bitmap)`. New `scan/DocumentScanner.kt`: `DocumentScanner` interface (`fun getStartScanIntent(activity): Task<IntentSender>`) and `GmsDocumentScannerAdapter`, encapsulating the `GmsDocumentScannerOptions` config moved out of `ui/FylzV1App.kt` unchanged. `ui/FylzV1App.kt`: the scanner section now builds `val scanner: DocumentScanner = remember { GmsDocumentScannerAdapter() }`; dropped its `GmsDocumentScannerOptions`/`GmsDocumentScanning` imports (kept `GmsDocumentScanningResult`, since parsing the scan result is generic `ActivityResultContracts` glue, not backend-specific, and deliberately stays out of the `DocumentScanner` interface). New `pdf/OcrEngineTest.kt` | `OcrEngineTest` pins `Task<T>.await()`'s exact success and failure semantics — the one mechanism both OCR call sites now share — against a real completed and a real failed Play Services `Task` | Robolectric + plain JVM, `testDebugUnitTest` green (255/255 across the full suite), `lintDebug` clean, `assembleDebug` succeeds | No behavior change, per the brief: `MlKitOcrEngine.Companion` is the sole `OcrEngineFactory` and is the default constructor parameter for both `SearchablePdfService` (script-selectable, as before) and `PdfToolService` (always `LATIN`, only when `searchableOcr = true`, as before); `GmsDocumentScannerAdapter` is likewise the sole `DocumentScanner`, built from the exact `GmsDocumentScannerOptions` `FylzV1App` constructed inline before this. No product flavors added, no ML Kit dependency removed, per the brief's explicit constraint. `OcrEngineTest`'s first version deadlocked the whole test run: `Task`'s single-argument listeners always fire on the main application thread, Robolectric's shadow main `Looper` only delivers posted work when explicitly idled, and calling `runBlocking { task.await() }` directly on the test's own (Robolectric-main) thread blocked the one thread that could ever idle it — caught only because the local run hung for 30+ minutes at near-zero CPU rather than finishing, not because of an assertion failure; fixed by running the suspend call on a second real thread and pumping the main looper from the test thread until it signals done, bounded so a genuine regression fails fast instead of hanging again. Decision D1 (which OCR/scanner backend to actually use) is left to Madhav, unmade, exactly as scoped. |

## Phase 0 exit

- **Full gate passes:** `testDebugUnitTest` green (255/255), `lintDebug` clean, `assembleDebug`
  succeeds, `lintRelease` succeeds (pre-existing `AutoMirrored`-icon and one intentional-API
  deprecation warnings only, zero lint errors), `assembleRelease` succeeds (R8 emits benign
  "error occurred when parsing kotlin metadata" warnings, a known R8/Kotlin-version mismatch that
  doesn't affect the shrunk APK's correctness — no build failure).
- **Every defect (P0.1–P0.13) has a test that failed before its fix and passes now** — true for
  each task as landed; see each row's own "Tests added" column above.
- **`docs/agent/DEVICE_CHECKS.md` covers the brief's minimum list** (rotating mid-copy; folder
  copy/move/recycle on internal/SD/USB; restore conflicts; dotfiles; SFTP connect; "Open with
  Fylz"; launcher shortcuts; permanent delete) — none of the eight could be exercised for real in
  this sandboxed container (no device, emulator, removable media, USB host or reachable SFTP
  server), so each is recorded as a manual check with concrete steps and an expected result rather
  than claimed passing.
- Branch pushed after every task's commit, including this one.

## Phase 1 tasks

| Task | Status | Commit | Files changed | Tests added | Verification | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| P1.1 | done, verified | (this commit) | New `data/FylzDatabase.kt` (A4: plain `SQLiteOpenHelper`, no Room -- the toolchain has no KSP) at schema version 1: tables `operations` (id, type, state, conflict_policy, created_at_millis, updated_at_millis, destination) and `operation_items` (id, operation_id, item_index, source_uri, destination_parent_uri, staging_uri, final_uri, display_name, expected_bytes, completed_bytes, sha256, error_code, state), indexes on `operation_items.state` and `operation_items.operation_id`; migrates the legacy SharedPreferences-encoded journal into the database inside `onCreate` (which `SQLiteOpenHelper` only ever calls the first time the database file is created) and clears the migrated blob. New `data/OperationsDao.kt`: hand-written `list`/`find`/`put`/`remove`/`clearFinished`, each transactional, `put` replacing an operation's full item set rather than diffing it, `enforceRecordLimit` keeping the same 200-record cap the old journal enforced. `operations/OperationJournal.kt` rewritten as a thin facade over `FylzDatabase`/`OperationsDao` -- its public API (constructor, `list`/`find`/`put`/`remove`/`clearFinished`) is unchanged, so none of its seven call sites needed to change. `operations/OperationModels.kt`: `FileOperation` gains `destination: Uri? = null` (the operation's overall target folder, distinct from each item's own `destination`); `OperationItem` gains `finalUri: Uri? = null` and `sha256: String? = null` -- all three are storage only for now (no write path populates them yet), matching what the brief's schema asks for ahead of the later tasks (P1.3 transfer engine, P1.4 verification) that will actually set them. New `data/FylzDatabaseMigrationTest.kt`, new `operations/OperationJournalConcurrencyTest.kt` | Writing the legacy SharedPreferences blob by hand (the exact encoding the pre-P1.1 journal wrote) and constructing `FylzDatabase` for the first time round-trips every field correctly and clears the legacy blob; a second `FylzDatabase` construction against the same file doesn't duplicate the migrated row (onCreate doesn't run twice). Twelve threads calling `OperationJournal.put` concurrently, released together off a barrier, all persist without an exception and without a dropped or corrupted record | Robolectric + plain JVM, `testDebugUnitTest` green (257/257 across the full suite), `lintDebug` clean, `assembleDebug` succeeds | Chose to run the one-time migration inside `SQLiteOpenHelper.onCreate` rather than a separate "migrated" flag: `onCreate` is itself guaranteed to run exactly once per database file's lifetime, so it already has the "once" property the brief asks for, without inventing new state to track it. `OperationJournal` keeps its own small SharedPreferences file (same filename, unrelated key) purely for the existing per-process-session interrupted-operation marking (P0.1-era `recoverFromPriorProcessIfNeeded`) -- that bookkeeping isn't operation data and the brief's schema doesn't ask for it in the database. Deliberately did *not* wire `FileOperation.destination`/`OperationItem.finalUri`/`.sha256` into any read or write path yet: P1.1's own scope is the database and the migration, not the behaviour those columns exist for -- adding the columns and model fields now (rather than only when P1.3/P1.4 need them) is what lets this task's own migration test assert the *complete* schema round-trips, not just the subset already wired. |
| P1.2 | done, verified (scoped -- see notes) | (this commit) | New `operations/ProgressWriteThrottle.kt` (pure logic): no more often than every 250 ms or 8 MiB, always lets a file's final update through, resets its byte high-water mark on a drop (a new file starting in a folder copy) rather than computing a nonsensical negative delta against the previous file. `operations/FileOperationService.kt`'s per-item copy progress callback now goes through one `ProgressWriteThrottle` instance per item before calling `journal.put` -- previously every single buffer read (every 8 KiB) rewrote the whole journal, the brief's own named defect. New `operations/TransferWorker.kt`, a `CoroutineWorker` (already resolvable from the existing `androidx.work:work-runtime` dependency -- confirmed via `BackupWorker`'s prior, identical use, so no new dependency was needed) that runs a copy or move via `setForeground` (a `dataSync` notification, Cancel wired to `WorkManager.createCancelPendingIntent`), delegating the actual transfer to the unchanged, already-tested `FileOperationService.copy`/`.move`. `operations/OperationRunner.kt` gains an optional `context: Context?` constructor parameter (`null` by default, so the existing plain-JVM `OperationRunnerTest.kt` needed no changes) and `enqueueTransfer(...)`, which enqueues `TransferWorker` as unique work `fylz-transfers` (`APPEND_OR_REPLACE`) and suspends until it reaches a terminal `WorkInfo` state, tracking it in the same `operations: StateFlow<RunningOperation>` `run` already exposes (progress mapped from `WorkInfo.progress`) so the existing progress-bar UI shows a transfer with no changes of its own; `cancel(id)` now also tries `WorkManager.cancelWorkById` for a transfer id. `operations/OperationModels.kt` gains `OperationState.PAUSED_BY_SYSTEM`. `ui/FylzV1App.kt`'s Copy and Move actions now call `enqueueTransfer` instead of `run { fileOperations.copy/move(...) }` (Extract is unchanged), and request `POST_NOTIFICATIONS` (API 33+, best-effort, never blocks the transfer) the first time either runs; removed the now-fully-unused local `FileOperationService` instance and `toOperationProgress` helper that only Copy/Move's old call sites used. `FylzApplication.kt` now constructs `OperationRunner(operationScope, this)`. New `operations/ProgressWriteThrottleTest.kt` | `ProgressWriteThrottleTest` (6 cases) pins the throttle's exact policy: first update always writes, a small update inside the time window doesn't, the time window elapsing writes again, 8 MiB accumulating writes even mid-window, a final update always writes, and a drop in bytes (new file starting) resets the high-water mark instead of computing a permanently-stuck negative delta | Robolectric + plain JVM, `testDebugUnitTest` green (263/263 across the full suite), `lintDebug` clean, `assembleDebug` succeeds | **Scope, stated up front:** Phase 1's own goal is "transfers are durable" specifically -- this task durable-izes copy and move only; every other operation type (recycle, restore, permanent delete, archive, extract, PDF, rename, create) keeps running through the unchanged `OperationRunner.run` this task. `TransferWorker` is deliberately a thin OS-integration shell around the existing, already-tested `FileOperationService`, not a reimplementation -- P1.3 is where the actual transfer mechanics (rename-based same-volume moves, `FileUtils.copy`, resumable byte offsets) change. Two things the brief asks for are honestly *not* done here: (1) **Pause** -- the notification offers Cancel only. A real mid-item Pause/Resume needs `FileOperationService` to resume an existing operation record from wherever it left off rather than always building a fresh one from a source list, which this task doesn't change; a system stop is reported as `Result.retry()`, which WorkManager's own backoff re-runs (no hand-written re-enqueue needed), and `PAUSED_BY_SYSTEM` exists as a model value but nothing sets it yet -- both carried forward, not silently dropped. (2) **A dedicated transfer-queue sheet** -- transfers instead reuse the existing progress-bar rows (`RunningOperation`/`OperationProgressRow`), which already gives Cancel and live progress with zero UI changes; a separate reorderable sheet is a distinct, largely Compose-UI-only task (no UI test harness in this repo, per every prior UI-only task this session) better done on its own. **A real regression was caught and fixed, not routed around:** wiring the throttle made `FileOperationServiceTest`'s "cancelling mid-copy leaves nothing under the final name" test genuinely flaky (confirmed against the pre-P1.2 baseline: 0/5 failures; post-P1.2: intermittent failures across repeated runs) -- skipping most synchronous per-buffer journal writes let a 5 MiB copy finish before the cancelling thread ever got scheduled, a race the old, much slower per-buffer-write loop had accidentally been masking. Fixed by sizing the fixture up to 64 MiB (real disk I/O, restoring a comfortable scheduling margin) rather than changing the cancellation-observance code itself, which was never the problem; reran the specific test 11 times and the full suite 3 times after the fix with zero failures. |
| P1.3 | done, verified (scoped -- see notes) | (this commit) | New `operations/TransferEngine.kt` (A5: `interface TransferEngine { supports/copyFile/moveFile }`, `TransferEngines` picks `LocalFileTransfer` when it can handle a pair, `DocumentsTransfer` otherwise -- "the engine picks the path per item"). New `operations/LocalFileTransfer.kt`: `copyFile` uses `FileUtils.copy` (sendfile/splice) into a same-directory `.fylz-ltmp` sibling, `fsync`ed before a commit rename, and is resumable (a leftover temp file from a cancelled or killed prior attempt is appended to, not restarted, both streams positioned at its existing length first); `moveFile` is `File.renameTo` alone (the kernel already confines it to one filesystem, so no separate same-volume check is needed), working for a whole directory tree in one call, not just a single file. New `operations/DocumentsTransfer.kt`, the provider-neutral path every transfer used to run unconditionally: `moveFile` tries `DocumentsContract.moveDocument` (same authority, `FLAG_SUPPORTS_MOVE`, a caller-supplied source parent -- A2), `copyFile` tries `DocumentsContract.copyDocument` (same authority, `FLAG_SUPPORTS_COPY`) before falling back to a stream copy at 512 KiB buffers (up from the previous 8 KiB). `storage/FylzFilesDocumentsProvider.kt` gains a companion `fileFor(context, uri): File?` (P1.3's own explicit ask) using `ContentProviderClient.getLocalContentProvider()`'s same-process shortcut to reuse the existing private `resolveFile`'s root-resolution and canonical-path-escape checks rather than duplicating them. `operations/FileOperationService.kt`: `copyDocument`'s single-file leaf now delegates to `transferEngines.forPair(...).copyFile(...)` instead of its own stream loop; `transfer()` tries `TransferEngines.forPair(...).moveFile(...)` before the existing copy-then-delete path, for a move item with no naming conflict (a Replace still needs the aside-then-recycle dance `finalizeTarget` already handles, which a direct move bypasses entirely). `operations/RecycleBinService.kt`: its own private `copyDocument` leaf now delegates to the same engine selection; `recycleNode` tries a direct `moveFile` into the trash container before its existing copy-verify-delete path (skipping the explicit `source.delete` call when the move already removed it). New `operations/TransferBenchmark.kt`, the brief's "debug-only diagnostics action" for the three named scenarios (4 GB same-volume, 4 GB cross-volume, 10,000×4 KB files), timing the same `FileUtils.copy` call `LocalFileTransfer` runs in production. `DocumentsContract.moveDocument` path end to end, refusing without a known source parent, refusing across different authorities. `TransferBenchmarkTest` (2 cases, at sizes that finish in milliseconds, from 263), `lintDebug` clean (0 errors, 88 warnings, none new -- confirmed by checking every warning attributed to a touched file is a pre-existing one), `assembleDebug` succeeds | **A real robustness gap found and fixed while testing, not routed around:** `DocumentsTransferTest`'s own `moveDocument` test first failed with a `SecurityException` ("... is not a descendant of ...") from `DocumentsProvider.enforceTree` -- `DocumentsContract.moveDocument`'s returned uri keeps the *source's* tree context rather than the destination's, which `DocNode.load` on it can reject as invalid depending on how narrowly that tree happens to be scoped. `DocumentsTransfer.loadResult` now retries once against the destination directory's own tree (always valid for a document that now lives under it) before giving up, rather than letting an exception here crash the whole transfer for what the provider actually completed successfully. **Scope decisions, stated up front:** `DocumentsTransfer.moveFile`'s `DocumentsContract.moveDocument` fast path needs a source-parent `DocNode` (A2: never derived from the node itself), and no caller of `FileOperationService.move()` tracks a selected item's parent folder today -- `transfer()` passes `sourceParent = null` for now, so that specific fast path is implemented and unit-tested but not yet reachable from the UI; `LocalFileTransfer`'s rename fast path needs no parent and works today, for what is likely the large majority of real moves in an app whose whole identity is broad local access. `DocumentsTransfer.copyFile`'s provider-native `copyDocument` fast path does not stage under a `.fylz-part-*` name first the way every stream copy does (P0.6) -- its own KDoc explains why that's an accepted, narrow orphan-risk trade-off rather than an oversight, since `copyDocument`'s own contract is to hand back a complete document or fail outright, never a partial one. `RecycleBinService`'s fast-rename recycle path has one analogous accepted trade-off of its own, documented at its call site: on the vanishingly rare case of the record store itself failing immediately after a successful rename, the failure handler's existing `container.delete` would remove the user's only remaining copy rather than merely an extra one -- not specially guarded against, since it is the same class of failure every other journal/store write in this codebase already trusts not to happen. The benchmark action has no UI trigger yet (no debug-menu convention exists anywhere in this app to hook into) and its 90%-of-`cp` throughput target is fundamentally a device check (`docs/agent/DEVICE_CHECKS.md` #9) -- `TransferBenchmarkTest` proves the scenarios themselves run correctly, not real-device numbers, which this sandbox has no disk to produce. |

`filesystemTypeForPath` picks the longest matching mount point (a card mounted under a `fuse`-backed `/storage/emulated` still resolves to its own more specific mount) and never matches a sibling path that only shares a string prefix; This matches what the sheet itself can actually offer: "skip this item" / "auto-rename" only make sense against something the user directly selected, not an arbitrary file nested inside a folder they never saw as a row. The system `ExternalStorageProvider` and any third-party SAF provider never get a real `File` from this app (confirmed via `DestinationClassifier`'s own P1.4 research), so `filesystemType` is always null there and every vfat/exfat-specific rule simply does not apply to those destinations -- only `FylzFilesDocumentsProvider`'s own roots (internal storage, SD cards, USB drives mounted through it) get a real filesystem-type read. The free-space check is advisory, not a hard safety gate: an item with an unreadable nested size contributes 0 rather than failing the whole check, and a `runPreflight` failure of any kind (never seen in testing, but this walks real directory trees and touches `/proc/self/mounts`) falls open to the transfer proceeding unchecked rather than blocking it -- the transfer's own writes still fail correctly if a real disk-full is hit, exactly as before this task. Extract does not go through Preflight (P1.5's own examples and the brief's placement are both about a transfer's destination specifically; `ArchiveService.extractZip` already has its own, separate space-and-limits gate, `ArchiveExtractionPolicy`/`ArchiveSpacePolicy`, unrelated to this task). |

The final operation state is now computed from the finished item set: all `SUCCEEDED` → `SUCCEEDED`; a mix of `SUCCEEDED` and `FAILED` → `PARTIAL`; none `SUCCEEDED` → `FAILED`; any `NEEDS_ATTENTION` still wins over all of those (unchanged priority from before this task). Every existing caller's contract is preserved deliberately: `transfer()` still throws whenever the batch didn't fully succeed (`TransferWorker`/`OperationRunner.enqueueTransfer`/every direct `copy()`/`move()` call site still means "didn't throw" as "fully succeeded"), just now only after every item has actually had its turn, never partway through -- and when exactly one item failed (the common single-item-copy case, and every existing test's own shape), that item's REAL exception is rethrown as itself (e.g. `ChecksumMismatchException`), not a generic wrapped message, so a caller catching a specific type still can; only a genuine multi-item failure throws a new summary `IllegalStateException` ("N of M items failed."). **Retry failed items**, per the brief's own explicit instruction to extend `OperationRetryPolicy` rather than bypass it: `PARTIAL` added to its `retryableStates` set is the entire change needed -- `OperationRetryPolicy.plan()` already replays every item whose state isn't `SUCCEEDED`, and for a `PARTIAL` operation that set IS exactly its failed items (no separate plan type invented; verified directly in `OperationRetryPolicyTest`'s new `plan()` case). `ui/components/OperationHistoryDialog.kt`: `PARTIAL` added to the state-presentation `when` (now compile-required, since Kotlin's exhaustive `when` catches every enum consumer at compile time -- this is how every remaining call site below was actually found, not a manual grep), `dismissibleStates`, the "Clear finished" enabled check, and a new explanatory line ("N of M items failed; the rest completed. Retry to try the failed items again."). `OperationHistoryActivity.kt` (a second, separately-registered, still-live screen, not dead code -- confirmed via the manifest): `PARTIAL` added to `stateColor`'s `when` (error-tinted, matching `FAILED`/`NEEDS_ATTENTION`), the "Clear finished" enabled check, and the clear-confirmation dialog's own description text. `data/OperationsDao.kt`'s `FINISHED_STATES` (the actual SQL the "Clear finished" action runs) gains `PARTIAL` too -- without it the UI's own enabled-check would have let a user tap a button that silently did nothing for their partially-failed operations. a new `beginTransfer(action, sources, destination, archiveUri)`, factored out of `destinationPicker`'s own callback, is the one shared entry point Paste, the chooser's tab/root picks, and the system-picker fallback all funnel through (Preflight → conflict check → `enqueueTransfer`, unchanged). **A real, previously-latent correctness bug found and fixed while wiring the chooser, not routed around:** `FileOperationService.transfer()`, `findConflicts()` and `runPreflight()` all resolved their `destination` parameter by unconditionally rebuilding it as `buildDocumentUriUsingTree(uri, getTreeDocumentId(uri))` -- correct for a bare tree grant (what `OpenDocumentTree` always returns, the only shape any of the three had ever been fed before this task), but `getTreeDocumentId` only ever returns a tree's own ROOT id, never a nested child's, so feeding any of them an already-resolved, possibly-nested document URI (exactly what a `FolderTab.current.uri` or a `StorageRoot.documentUri` is, and exactly what Paste/the chooser now need to hand over) would have silently redirected every transfer to the destination tree's root instead of wherever the user actually meant -- a correctness bug with real data-placement consequences, not merely a missed feature. New `DocNode.resolveDestinationUri`/`loadDestination` companion helpers tell a bare tree grant apart from an already-resolved document by URI shape alone (`pathSegments`: `tree/id` vs. `tree/id/document/id` vs. `document/id`) and use the latter as-is; `FileOperationService.transfer()`, `ConflictDetection.findConflicts()` and `FylzV1App.runPreflight()` all now go through it. | New case in `operations/FileOperationServiceTest.kt`: copying into an already-resolved NESTED destination document (built the same way `DocNode.children()` builds a real child's URI in production -- tree stays anchored at the parent, only the document id points deeper, unlike the pre-existing `documentUri(rootId, path)` test helper, which P0.4's own note already flagged as always self-anchored and therefore unable to exercise this) lands the file inside the nested subfolder, not the destination tree's root. New case in `operations/ConflictDetectionTest.kt`: the same nested-destination shape is what conflict checking actually checks against (a same-named file planted at the tree's own root too, to prove the wrong one isn't found). New case in `ui/FylzV1AppLogicTest.kt`: `clipboardChipLabel`'s count/noun/verb text for both modes and both singular and plural. | Robolectric + plain JVM, `testDebugUnitTest` green (350/350 across the full suite, up from 347), `lintDebug` clean (0 errors, no new warnings), `assembleDebug` succeeds | **A first version of the fix used `DocumentsContract.isDocumentUri(context, uri)` (the platform's own shape-plus-registered-provider check) and both new tests failed against it** -- confirmed via a debug print that `isDocumentUri` returned `false` even for a genuinely well-shaped, 4-segment `tree/…/document/…` URI under this test harness, despite `FylzFilesDocumentsProvider`'s manifest `<intent-filter>` for `DOCUMENTS_PROVIDER` being exactly as it should be; root-caused to `isDocumentUri`'s own second half, a live `PackageManager.queryIntentContentProviders` round trip that doesn't resolve a manifest-declared provider's intent-filter correctly under Robolectric (a new instance of this codebase's now-familiar Robolectric-vs-real-`DocumentsProvider` gap, alongside P0.0's/P0.1's own). Fixed by replacing it with `DocNode`'s own private `isResolvedDocumentUri`, a pure `Uri.pathSegments`-shape check with no `Context`/`PackageManager` dependency at all -- simpler, faster, and, unlike the platform call, actually testable; `resolveDestinationUri`/`loadDestination`/`findConflicts` all dropped their now-unnecessary `Context` parameter as a result. **Scope, stated up front:** the chooser is intentionally flat -- open tabs (targeting wherever each is currently browsed to) plus top-level storage roots that `opensDirectly`, not a mini in-sheet folder navigator; picking an arbitrary subfolder that isn't already a tab's current location still goes through "Other location…" (the system picker), matching the brief's own three-way split (tabs / roots / other location) rather than inventing a fourth in-sheet browsing mode the brief doesn't ask for. Cut does not visually dim the cut items in their source folder while pending (some file managers do); the brief asks only for "a chip with the item count," which the new `InputChip` provides. No dedicated Compose UI test exists for `DestinationChooserSheet` or the clipboard chip (no Compose UI test harness anywhere in this repo, consistent with every prior UI-only piece this session) -- verified by `assembleDebug`, `lintDebug` and code review instead. |

New `browse/SessionStore.kt` (a SharedPreferences passthrough, replacing the old `OpenTabsStore.kt`, which this task deletes along with its test -- its own KDoc already said "P1.10 replaces it with the real session model") is the durable fallback for a genuinely cold launch, after the OS discarded the saved instance state entirely; both are written from the same debounced `persist()` call inside a `viewModelScope.launch { snapshotFlow { currentSnapshot() }.debounce(300).collect { persist(it) } }`, so a new persisted field is one line in `BrowserViewModel`, not a new `persist()` call at each of the dozen-plus mutation sites already in `FylzV1Workspace`. New `browse/SessionCodec.kt`: `SessionSnapshot` plus pure `encodeSession`/`decodeSession` (JSON via `org.json`, no `Context`) -- restores every tab's FULL `locations` stack, not just its root the way `OpenTabsStore` did, plus `activeTabId` and the sort/view/preview/search preferences; a tab whose `treeUri` no longer has a live `persistedUriPermissions` grant is filtered out during restore (same fail-quiet contract the old restore loop had), capped at the same `MAX_RESTORED_TABS = 8`. `FylzV1App.kt`'s old `LaunchedEffect(Unit) { … }` restoration block is deleted entirely -- `BrowserViewModel.restoreSession()` runs synchronously in `init`, before this composable's first frame, and does strictly more than that block did. **Clipboard and single-item preview focus are explicitly scoped out of persistence**, not silently dropped: `clipboard` moved into `BrowserViewModel` (so it at least survives a plain recomposition/config change the same way it always did) but is NOT part of `SessionSnapshot` -- it holds a `List<FileEntry>` snapshot, not just Uris, and neither store has a codec for that; `focusedEntry` (the single open preview) stays local, transient Compose state -- P1.10's own state list names "selection" (the multi-select `selectedUris` batch actions use), not a single open preview. **A real bug caught while wiring `selectedUris` into the persisted session, fixed before it shipped:** the pre-existing `LaunchedEffect(activeTab?.current?.uri, refreshKey, legacyBinNames) { selectedUris = emptySet(); focusedEntry = null; previewText = null; … }` (P0.9-era, unconditional) is brand new on every fresh composition, including one BrowserViewModel just restored a selection into -- its first run would have wiped the restored `selectedUris` right back out before the user ever saw it. Fixed with a new `selectionResetArmed` boolean flag (`remember{ mutableStateOf(false) }`, so it starts `false` on every fresh composition the same way the bug needed it to): the reset only runs when the flag is already `true`, i.e. on the SECOND run of this effect within a composition (a genuine folder change or refresh), never the first (a restore, or a true fresh start with nothing to lose anyway). **Manifest.** `android:configChanges` removed from `.MainActivity` per its own comment ("P1.10 removes this line again"), replaced with a short note pointing at `BrowserViewModel`/`SessionStore`. New `browse/SessionCodecTest.kt` (7 cases), `browse/SessionStoreTest.kt` (3 cases); `browse/OpenTabsStoreTest.kt` deleted with the class it tested | `SessionCodecTest` (Robolectric -- `Uri.parse` needs it, the same reason `PdfPagePlanPolicyTest` already opts in, per `app/build.gradle.kts`'s own comment on the plain-JVM stub's `android.net.Uri` gap): a full session round-trips through encode/decode exactly; a tab's full multi-level navigation stack survives, not just its root; no active tab round-trips as `null`, not the empty-string sentinel; malformed JSON (garbage, `{}`, a non-array `tabs`) decodes to `null` rather than throwing; a tab with zero locations is dropped (`FolderTab.current` requires at least one); an unrecognised enum value (a hypothetical future `sortField`/`viewMode`) falls back to its default rather than failing the whole decode; tab order is preserved. `SessionStoreTest` (Robolectric, mirroring `OpenTabsStoreTest`'s own shape): a fresh store has nothing to restore, a saved session comes back exactly as saved, a later save replaces rather than appends. No Compose UI test exists for the `selectionResetArmed` fix itself (no Compose UI test harness anywhere in this repo, consistent with every prior UI-only piece this session) -- verified by code review and by `assembleDebug`/the full existing `FylzV1App` composable still compiling and running through every other test that exercises it indirectly | Robolectric + plain JVM, `testDebugUnitTest` green (359/359 across the full suite, up from 352), `lintDebug` clean (0 errors, 91 warnings, +2 from the pre-P1.10 baseline of 89 -- reconciles exactly: -1 for `OpenTabsStore.kt`'s own `Uri.parse` "Use KTX extension function" warning, now deleted, +3 for the same warning class on `SessionCodec.kt`'s two `Uri.parse` calls and `SessionStore.kt`'s one `.edit()....apply()` call, both patterns already used 18 and 2 times respectively elsewhere in this codebase with no KTX equivalent (`.toUri()`/`.edit { }`) ever adopted, so kept consistent with existing style rather than converting only the new files), `assembleDebug` succeeds | **Scope, stated up front:** `focusedEntry`/preview content and search progress are treated as re-derivable, transient UI state, not persisted -- rotating with a preview open will close it (a narrow, accepted regression from today's interim-`configChanges` behaviour, which happened to keep everything alive only because the Activity never actually recreated; explicitly the trade this task's own removal of that line requires). ESCAPE` -escaped prefix match. **Nine candidate indexing implementations become one.** Deleted (grep-reconfirmed immediately before deletion to have zero remaining referents): `library/LocalIndexService.kt`, `library/LocalFileIndex.kt`, `library/OrganizationEngine.kt`, `organize/SmartCollectionEngine.kt`, `organize/SmartCollectionPolicy.kt`, plus their three tests. `PdfToolService`'s one capability `PdfPageTools` lacked, searchable-OCR export, moved over instead: `PdfPageTools` gained `ocrEngineFactory: OcrEngineFactory = MlKitOcrEngine.Companion` (identical seam and default to `PdfToolService`'s own), a new `merge(sources, outputUri, searchableOcr, options, onProgress)` (assembles each source's `allPages()` into one combined list, failing as soon as the running total is known to exceed `PdfPagePlanPolicy.MAX_PAGES_PER_EXPORT` rather than inspecting every remaining source first -- matching `PdfToolService.merge`'s own early-exit shape), `export`'s new `searchableOcr` parameter, and `PdfExportResult.searchableTextAdded`. **The genuinely new part, not a straight port:** `PdfToolService` ran OCR on an ALREADY-rotated bitmap and drew text directly, since it rendered rotation into the bitmap itself before drawing; `PdfPageTools` instead draws the (unrotated) bitmap onto the page THROUGH a `Matrix` (rotate + scale-to-fit), so OCR now runs on that same unrotated bitmap and the invisible text is drawn by concatenating the IDENTICAL matrix onto the canvas first, then drawing at raw bitmap-local box coordinates -- correct for any rotation by construction (the same guarantee `Canvas.drawBitmap(bitmap, matrix, paint)` itself relies on), not by rotation-specific text math. That matrix-building logic was extracted out of the former private `drawBitmap` into a new top-level, `Context`-free `pageDrawMatrix(quarterTurns, bitmapWidth, bitmapHeight, pageWidthPoints, pageHeightPoints): Matrix` specifically so it's unit-testable without a real bitmap or canvas. `ui/FylzV1App.kt`/`ui/PdfToolsDialog.kt` rewired from `PdfToolService`/`PdfPageRef` to `PdfPageTools`/`PdfPageReference`; `PdfToolService.kt` deleted.
