`compileDebugKotlin`/`compileDebugAidl` clean, confirming the AIDL-generated `IDecoderService`/`IDecoderService.Stub` types are usable both from `DecoderService`'s real binder implementation and from the test's fakes | **A real, empirically-found bug in the test strategy itself, not in `DecoderClient`.** The first version of `DecoderClientTest` used `kotlinx.coroutines.test.runTest` (added as a new `kotlinx-coroutines-test` test dependency) to drive the suspend calls, reasoning that its virtual clock would make a "timeout fires, next call succeeds instantly" test fast and deterministic. It was neither: 3 of 8 cases failed, including the trivial "an instant binder's `ping()` succeeds" case running through the SAME `withTimeoutOrNull` wrapper a slow one uses. Root-caused by instrumenting `call()` with temporary debug prints and re-running: `runTest`'s scheduler auto-advances its virtual clock to the next scheduled event the instant the test dispatcher has no queued work of its own -- and `withContext(Dispatchers.IO) { block(service) }` (deliberately real threading, matching how an actual blocking AIDL/Binder call must run) leaves the test dispatcher with nothing queued the moment it dispatches, so the scheduler raced the scheduled timeout callback forward and fired it in every case, independent of how fast the real IO-dispatched work actually finished -- an inherent mismatch between `runTest`'s virtual time and a coroutine that genuinely crosses onto a real dispatcher mid-flight, not a flaky timing coincidence (reproduced identically across two separate runs). Fixed by switching every test to plain `kotlinx.coroutines.runBlocking` (real time, no scheduler racing ahead of real dispatched work) and dropping the `kotlinx-coroutines-test` dependency entirely -- the real wall-clock cost is trivial (a 20 ms configured timeout, hanging fakes capped at 500 ms), and `DecoderClient`'s own production code was never the problem. **Scope, stated up front:** `DecoderClient`'s timeout defaults (`DEFAULT_TIMEOUT_MILLIS`) are a single generic value, not yet the purpose-specific 5 s (thumbnails) / 30 s (structure) section 4.4 names -- there is exactly one trivial caller today (nothing calls `sniff` from app code yet), so a single default is honest; a real caller choosing its own `timeoutMillis` is M2.5-or-later's job. The "256 MB address-space target" and real per-process resource limits are unenforced by anything in this task (Android has no direct manifest knob for it); tracked as a stated gap, not silently dropped. Genuine cross-process kill-on-timeout, a real crash not reaching the host app, and confirming `:decoders` truly has no inherited permissions are all real-device-only and were not claimed as verified here -- see `DEVICE_CHECKS.md` §14. **Corrected in `c9b184a` (found by the M3.2 design review, 2026-09-25):** `DecoderClient`'s per-call timeout wrapped `withContext(Dispatchers.IO) { binderCall }`, and structured concurrency waits for that blocking body, so `withTimeoutOrNull` returned `null` only after the Binder call itself returned -- a hung `:decoders` was never unbound and the caller's coroutine was frozen for the length of the native call (measured: 0.52-0.58 s against a 500 ms sleeper under a 20 ms timeout). The transaction now runs in a client-owned `CoroutineScope(Dispatchers.IO + SupervisorJob())` job and the timeout wraps only the await; on timeout the client unbinds at once and the orphaned transaction is swallowed with one warning when it returns. Elapsed-time tests added (`DecoderClientTest` 8 -> 12 cases, app suite 425 -> 429): a 1,500 ms hang returns in ~101 ms. `ARCHITECTURE.md`'s decoder section states the corrected contract. Still device-only: that the platform actually reaps the unbound process (`DEVICE_CHECKS.md` section 14 step 4). |
| M2.5 | done, verified | (this commit) | **Content sniffing (`fylz-sniff`), real detection replacing M2.2's stub.** `shared-mime-info` itself is GPL-2.0-or-later (verified against its `COPYING` file), which `deny.toml` denies for an ordinary Cargo dependency, and copying its magic *rules* wholesale would still copy that project's own expression of them -- so per the master plan's own stated fallback, this hand-writes rules from each format's public specification instead. New `core/crates/fylz-sniff/src/lib.rs`: a `Format` enum (`label()`/`mime()`) and `sniff`/`sniff_with_tail(header, tail)` covering every format the plan names explicitly (ISO9660, UDF, MBR, GPT, DMG, VHD, VHDX, QCOW, VMDK, VDI, WIM, 7z, RAR4/5, ZIP, zstd, xz, bz2, gz, lz4, MPEG-TS, PDF, OOXML, ODF, EPUB, APK) plus common raster/executable/database formats (PNG/JPEG/GIF/BMP/WebP/TIFF, ELF/PE/Mach-O/Java-class, SQLite, JAR) -- nowhere near "the top 300," but a table-driven design meant to make growing toward that additive, not a restructure. Structural detail worth naming: GPT is checked ahead of MBR (a GPT disk still carries a protective MBR's `0x55AA`); UDF ahead of ISO9660 (real UDF media is always bridged with a `CD001` descriptor too); `0xCAFEBABE` disambiguates a Mach-O fat binary from a Java `.class` file by its `nfat_arch`-vs-major-version heuristic (the same one `file(1)` uses); ISO9660/UDF are found by scanning the Volume Recognition Sequence (sectors 16-20) for `CD001`/`BEA01`/`NSR02`/`NSR03`; DMG/VHD are the only trailer-only formats (`sniff_with_tail`'s `tail` parameter), everything else is header-only; ZIP-based container formats (APK/JAR/OOXML/EPUB/ODF) are identified by parsing just the first local-file-header's filename (and, for the OCF `mimetype` convention, its stored content) rather than a full central-directory walk. `core/crates/fylz-ffi-android/src/lib.rs`: `sniff(path_fd)`'s stub body is replaced with real detection -- `path_fd` is wrapped in `ManuallyDrop<File>` (load-bearing: an ordinary `File` would close this caller-owned descriptor on drop, which this function must never do), `format_from_reader<R: Read + Seek>` reads `fylz_sniff::HEADER_LEN` bytes from the start and, when the file is longer than `TAIL_LEN`, `TAIL_LEN` bytes from the end, generic over any `Read + Seek` so the actual read-and-sniff logic is unit-testable against an in-memory `Cursor` with no real fd, temp file or platform plumbing involved; the returned `String` is the format's MIME type where one exists, else its label, else `"unknown"` (a read failure or an unrecognised file is `"unknown"` too, never a panic or a thrown error the AIDL boundary has no channel for yet). `fylz-ffi-android/Cargo.toml` gained a `fylz-sniff = { path = "../fylz-sniff", version = "0.1.0" }` dependency (the explicit `version` isn't decorative -- `deny.toml`'s `[bans] wildcards = "deny"` rejects a bare-path dependency as unconstrained, caught by `cargo deny check` itself). Doc-comment fixups in three files whose text explicitly said "stub"/"M2.5 lands" and were now stale: `FylzCore.kt`, `DecoderService.kt`, `IDecoderService.aidl` -- no behavioural change, since `sniff`'s signature (`suspend fun sniffFile(pathFd: Int): String`) is unchanged end to end and `DecoderService`/`DecoderClient`/the AIDL interface needed no edits at all, exactly as M2.4's own note anticipated. `FileFormatRegistry` (the master plan's stated Kotlin-side consumer) does not exist yet and is not created here -- it is explicitly a later milestone's own scope (M4/M6.2), and this task's job was `fylz-sniff` plus the decoder-service wiring, not inventing that registry early | New `core/crates/fylz-sniff/src/lib.rs` `#[cfg(test)] mod tests` (17 cases): every fixed-prefix format round-trips on a synthetic byte buffer; RAR5's 8-byte magic is not misdetected as RAR4's shorter, overlapping 7-byte one; WebP requires both `RIFF` and the `WEBP` fourcc (a plain WAV's `RIFF` header alone must not match); all four thin Mach-O magics match; `0xCAFEBABE` correctly resolves to `MachOFat` for a small `nfat_arch` and `JavaClass` for a realistic major-version value; MPEG-TS needs three synced `0x47` packets 188 bytes apart, not one stray byte; VDI matches its offset-64 signature; ISO9660 matches `CD001` at any of its three documented sectors; UDF wins over the ISO9660 bridge descriptor every real UDF disc also carries; GPT wins over the protective MBR signature every GPT disk also carries; MBR matches only once nothing more specific has; DMG and VHD are recognised from the tail when the header alone gives no answer, and a dynamic VHD's header-copy magic is recognised without needing the tail at all; every ZIP-container format (APK/JAR/OOXML/EPUB/ODF/plain-ZIP) is identified by a hand-built first-entry fixture, including a compressed entry named "mimetype" correctly NOT being read as an OCF declaration (the convention requires it stored); empty/garbage input matches nothing without panicking; every `Format` variant has a non-empty label. New `core/crates/fylz-ffi-android/src/lib.rs` tests (7 cases, up from 2): the negative-fd case now asserts `"unknown"` rather than the old stub string; an empty reader is `None`, not an error; a PNG header is identified by content; a file shorter than `TAIL_LEN` still gets its own bytes as a usable tail (the seek-back-from-end path doesn't panic or miss the file's own data); a DMG is identified only once its trailer is actually read; `detect_format` rejects a negative fd without touching the OS at all | `core/`: `cargo build/clippy --all-features --all-targets -- -D warnings/fmt --check/test --workspace/deny check` all green (24 new tests total, 0 failures; `cargo deny`'s `bans` check caught the real wildcard-dependency gap below before this reached a commit). `./gradlew --no-daemon :app:buildCoreDebug` succeeds (cross-compiles all three ABIs with the new dependency, `check16KbPageAlignment` passes); the generated `fylz_ffi_android.kt` is confirmed unchanged (`sniff`'s signature didn't move) with no diff to commit. `./gradlew --no-daemon :app:testDebugUnitTest :app:lintDebug` green: 393/393 (unchanged from M2.4 -- no Kotlin-side behavioural change, `sniff`'s contract is the same `String`, only what it returns changed on the Rust side), `lintDebug` clean (0 errors, 87 warnings, unchanged from M2.4's baseline; confirmed none of the three touched Kotlin/AIDL doc-only files contribute a finding) | **A real gap `cargo deny check` itself caught before this shipped, not a style choice:** the first version of `fylz-sniff = { path = "../fylz-sniff" }` failed `cargo deny check`'s `bans` check outright -- `error[wildcard]: found 1 wildcard dependency` -- since a path dependency with no `version` requirement is exactly what "wildcard" (an unconstrained, would-resolve-to-`*`-if-published dependency) means to cargo-deny, and `deny.toml`'s `[bans] wildcards = "deny"` (an M2.1 supply-chain hardening default) rejects it; fixed by adding the crate's own actual version alongside the path, the standard shape for a workspace-internal path dependency that could in principle be published. This is the FIRST internal (path-based) dependency anywhere in this workspace, so there was no existing convention to follow. **"Top 300 formats" is explicitly not attempted, and said so up front rather than overclaimed:** the master plan's own text conditions that number on `shared-mime-info`'s magic database, gated on a license check this task's own research resolved as "no" (GPL-2.0-or-later, confirmed against the upstream `COPYING` file) -- coverage here is the plan's own explicitly-named format list plus a reasonable common set for a file manager, roughly 39 formats, with the crate's own module doc stating plainly that growing further is a matter of adding table rows and fixtures, not a re-architecture. **Known, stated gaps, not silent ones:** an empty (`PK\x05\x06`-only) or spanned (`PK\x07\x08`-first) ZIP archive has no first local entry to name and falls through to `None` rather than a generic `Zip` guess -- documented in `sniff_zip_container`'s own doc rather than silently wrong; `Format::Ooxml` deliberately has no differentiated docx/xlsx/pptx variant or MIME type, since telling them apart needs more than the first ZIP entry's name; the PE/Mach-O/ELF checks are magic-only (no header-structure validation beyond the leading bytes), consistent with this crate's own stated scope ("magic-byte and container-structure sniffing, never a full parse"). **Read-only research, not assumption, decided the license question:** rather than guess at `shared-mime-info`'s license from memory, this task's first step was confirming it directly (GPL-2.0-or-later per its own `COPYING` file) before writing a single format rule, since guessing wrong in either direction -- assuming it was permissive when it wasn't, or hand-writing rules unnecessarily when a dependency would have been fine -- would have been the wrong kind of mistake to make silently. **`FileFormatRegistry` is a stated non-goal of this task**, not an oversight: the master plan names it as `fylz-sniff`'s eventual Kotlin-side consumer, but it belongs to M4/M6.2's own scope (an "ordered handler list" over MULTIPLE decoders, not just this one crate) and inventing an early, single-purpose version of it now would be exactly the kind of speculative abstraction this session's own workflow rules ask not to build. **GATE-M2 status:** M2.6 (CI: `cargo test`/`clippy`/`deny`/fuzz-smoke steps in the Android workflow) is the last item before GATE-M2's own APK-size/cold-start report to Madhav; Instead: (1) rustc bundles `static=` libraries into an rlib, and `llvm-ar t libfylz_archive-*.rlib` for each ABI lists zlib's CMake-built objects `deflate.c.o`/`inflate.c.o` (the NDK's `libz.a` members are named `adler32.o`-style, without `.c`) with `llvm-nm` showing `T deflate`/`T inflate` -- the objects come from the vendored `libz.a`, and no downstream link line ever sees `-lz`; (2) the only zlib version string in each rlib is `1.3.2` (the NDK sysroot's is `1.3.0.1-motley`, the host distro's `1.3`); (3) on the host, the test binary defines `inflate` itself, `ldd` shows no `libz.so`, and its only zlib version string is `1.3.2`. Not proven: a `cargo ndk … -vv` final link line -- an rlib-only build has no link step, so there is none yet; (1) makes its ordering moot as long as the link stays `static=`. `./gradlew --no-daemon :app:assembleDebug` green. **bzip2:** host gates green (`cargo test -p fylz-archive` 22/22, clippy `-D warnings`, fmt, deny -- `cc` 1.4.7 MIT OR Apache-2.0). Host `config.h`: `HAVE_LIBBZ2 1`, `HAVE_BZLIB_H 1`; `CMakeCache.txt`: `ENABLE_BZip2:BOOL=ON`, `BZIP2_INCLUDE_DIR` = `core/third_party/bzip2`, `BZIP2_LIBRARIES` = `$OUT_DIR/bzip2/libbz2.a`, `BZIP2_NEED_PREFIX:INTERNAL=1` (= FindBZip2's `CHECK_SYMBOL_EXISTS(BZ2_bzCompressInit)` link probe against our `.a` passed); configure log `Found BZip2: .../out/bzip2/libbz2.a (found version "1.0.8")`; `nm libbz2.a` defines `BZ2_bzCompressInit`/`BZ2_bzDecompress`/`BZ2_bzDecompressInit`/`BZ2_bzlibVersion` and the stdio API (`BZ2_bzRead`/`BZ2_bzWrite`/`BZ2_bzopen`, proving `BZ_NO_STDIO` is off) with no `bz_internal_error` reference; `nm libarchive.a` has 7 `U BZ2_*`; the rlib bundles the cc-built `*-blocksort.o`/`*-decompress.o`/`*-bzlib.o`. Android: `cargo ndk --platform 31 -t arm64-v8a -t armeabi-v7a -t x86_64 build --release -p fylz-archive` compiles all three ABIs; each ABI's `config.h` has both defines, its cache `BZIP2_LIBRARIES` = its own `libbz2.a` with `BZIP2_NEED_PREFIX=1` and `ANDROID_PLATFORM=android-31`, and `llvm-readelf -h` on each `libbz2.a` reports the matching machine (AArch64 / ARM ELF32 / X86-64), i.e. `cc` picked up cargo-ndk's `CC_<target>`/`AR_<target>` NDK toolchain rather than the host compiler. `./gradlew --no-daemon :app:assembleDebug` green. **xz:** host gates green (`cargo test -p fylz-archive` 26/26, clippy `-D warnings`, fmt, deny). | M3.1 (part 3: extraction policy, streaming extract) | done, verified (3a policy; 3b extract/inspect; host + three-ABI cross-compile) | `1f0a9a6` (3a), (this commit) (3b) | **3a (policy):** New `core/crates/fylz-archive/src/policy.rs`: `Limits` (the seven Kotlin `ArchiveExtractionLimits` fields with identical defaults -- 10,000 entries, 2 GiB archive, 1 GiB file, 4 GiB total, ratio 200.0, depth 64, name 255 -- plus `max_listing_entries` = 200,000, a decoder-process memory bound for 3b's `inspect`, not a policy rule), `EntryKind` (`File`/`Directory`/`Symlink`/`Hardlink`/`Other`), `EntryMetadata` (the design's section-6 shape: `path`, `name_lossy`, `kind`, `link_target`, `uncompressed: Option<u64>`, `compressed: Option<u64>`, `mtime: Option<i64>`, `mode`, `encrypted_data`, `encrypted_metadata`), `Decision { allowed, reason: Option<&'static str> }`, `evaluate(archive_bytes, &entries, &limits)`. Rule order and every reason string are the Kotlin file's verbatim: archive size, entry count, then per entry `validate_path` (blank, `maxNameLength*maxPathDepth` bound, NUL, leading `/`/`\`, backslash normalisation, empty-segment drop, depth, `.`/`..`, segment length, drive letter), duplicate `normalized_path_key`, unknown uncompressed size, file size, running total with a `checked_add` overflow guard, per-entry implausibly-compressed/ratio. Mappings as designed: a negative `archiveBytes` is unrepresentable in `u64`; `lowercase(Locale.ROOT)` -> `to_lowercase`; the drive-letter regex -> a two-byte check; Kotlin's `String.length` (UTF-16 units) and `isBlank` (Java whitespace: Zs/Zl/Zp + `\t`..`\r` + U+001C..U+001F) reproduced exactly rather than approximated with `len()`/`trim()`. Decision 1: `compressed == None` skips the two per-entry ratio rules; an archive-level ratio (sum of declared sizes / archive bytes > `max_compression_ratio`, a zero-length archive with declared bytes counting as infinite) runs after the loop with the same "suspicious compression ratio" reason. Section 6.2's link rule, after the entry's path rules and before its size rules: a `Symlink` whose target is absolute (leading slash after backslash normalisation, or a drive letter) or whose `..` segments climb above the root when joined onto `dirname(path)`, a `Hardlink` whose target fails `validate_path`, or either kind with no target -> "Archive contains a link that escapes the extraction folder."; links and `Other` count as files for the size rules. `normalized_path_key` is `pub(crate)` for 3b's `Selection::Paths`. `lib.rs`: `pub mod policy`, `#[cfg(test)] mod policy_tests`, module doc updated. New `core/fuzz/fuzz_targets/policy_evaluate.rs` (mirror types with `arbitrary` derives, field for field, so an engine-side type change fails the fuzz build; runs `evaluate` under fuzzed limits and under the defaults, asserting `allowed == reason.is_none()`); `core/fuzz/Cargo.toml`: package renamed `fylz-sniff-fuzz` -> `fylz-fuzz` (it now covers two crates), `libfuzzer-sys` gains its `arbitrary-derive` feature (no new direct dependency line; `derive_arbitrary`/`syn` enter only the fuzz workspace's own lock, never `core/Cargo.lock` or `deny.toml`'s graph), a `fylz-archive` path dependency, the new `[[bin]]`; `core/fuzz/README.md` documents both targets and the `fuzz build` gate. `.github/workflows/android.yml`: the fuzz smoke step now runs `cargo +nightly fuzz build` then 60 s each of `sniff` and `policy_evaluate`; job `timeout-minutes` 40 -> 50. `app/.../data/ArchiveExtractionPolicy.kt`: the one-line header naming `policy.rs` as the source of truth (decision 2: the Kotlin copy is deleted only when `ArchiveService`'s read path moves onto the engine -- M3.4 per the M3.2 design's scope note). **3b (extract/inspect):** `core/crates/fylz-archive/src/lib.rs`: `sys` gains 12 declarations (`archive_read_data_block`, `archive_read_has_encrypted_entries`, `archive_format`, `archive_format_name`, `archive_entry_hardlink`/`_symlink`/`_size_is_set`/`_perm`/`_mtime`/`_mtime_is_set`/`_is_data_encrypted`/`_is_metadata_encrypted`), each checked against `archive.h`/`archive_entry.h` at v3.8.9; `archive_entry_mtime` is declared `-> c_long` (`TimeT`), never `i64`, and widened by `widen_time` (`time_t` is 32-bit on armv7 bionic). `ArchiveError` gains `NotSeekable(String)`, `Unsupported(String)`, `LimitExceeded { entry, rule }` (rules `"listing"`, `"entries"`, `"file"`, `"declared"`, `"total"`). The shared `Reader::open_fd` now does `fstat` through `ManuallyDrop<File>::metadata` (no `libc` dependency; `std` carries the glibc/bionic `struct stat` layouts) -> `NotSeekable("not seekable (fifo|socket|character device|block device|directory)")` unless `S_ISREG`, then `lseek(fd, 0, SEEK_SET)` via `Seek::seek` -- the correctness requirement of `DESIGN-M32-SEEKABLE-PFD.md` section 2.1 -- then libarchive's open; `#[cfg(test)] Reader::open_unchecked` skips the check and ignores a failed rewind. `Unsupported` is classified in `classify_open_error` (libarchive runs format bidding inside `archive_read_open_fd`, not at the first `next_header` as section 6.1 supposed: `archive_format() == 0` **and** the message is `archive_read.c`'s own "Unrecognized archive format", both required so a filter failing on a badly truncated stream stays `Fatal`) and in `classify_header_error` for a 7-Zip archive whose header is encrypted (format family `7ZIP` and a message containing "encrypted"). `Reader::next_header` returns the raw entry pointer; `listing_entry` (strict UTF-8, for `entries`/`read_entry`) and `metadata` (lossy names + `name_lossy`; hardlink test **before** `archive_entry_filetype`; `EntryKind` from `AE_IFMT`; `uncompressed` from `size_is_set`; `compressed` always `None`; `mtime` widened; `mode` = `archive_entry_perm`; both encryption flags) build the two shapes. New public API: `Inspection { archive_bytes (fstat), format_code (`& ARCHIVE_FORMAT_BASE_MASK`, after the first header or EOF), format_name: Option<String>, filters ("none" dropped), has_encrypted_entries: Option<bool>, entries }`; `inspect(fd, &limits)` (one header pass, never `archive_read_data`; past `max_listing_entries` -> `LimitExceeded { rule: "listing" }`); `inspect_with_policy(fd, &limits) -> (Inspection, Decision)`; `Selection { All, Paths }`; `ExtractLimits` (+ `From<&Limits>`, `Default`); `DestinationProvider { open, done }`; `ExtractReport { entries_written, bytes_written, skipped, skipped_links, missing }`; `extract(fd, &selection, &limits, &mut dyn DestinationProvider)`: one forward pass, unselected entries passed over without reading data, `Selection::Paths` matched by `policy::normalized_path_key` with unmatched requests in `missing`, `max_entries` counted over selected entries, `Symlink`/`Hardlink` never offered (`skipped_links`), `Other` never offered (`skipped`), directories offered, data streamed with `archive_read_data_block` (zero copy) into the provider's `BorrowedFd` through a `ManuallyDrop<File>` (written and flushed, never closed), every block accounted **before** it is written against `min(max_file_bytes, declared size)` (rules `"file"`/`"declared"`) and the running total (`"total"`), sparse holes (a block offset past what was written, or the EOF block's offset) written as zeros so the output is byte-exact and sized as declared, holes counted against the caps. `Decision`/`EntryKind`/`EntryMetadata`/`Limits` re-exported at the crate root; the existing 26 tests keep their helpers (`build_ar_fixture`, `open_fixture`, `tempdir` now `pub(crate)`) and `a_corrupt_file_is_a_fatal_error_not_a_panic` becomes `a_non_archive_is_unsupported_not_a_panic`. Three-ABI `cargo ndk --platform 31 -t arm64-v8a -t armeabi-v7a -t x86_64 build --release -p fylz-archive` green for all three (the `-> c_long` `time_t` declaration and `widen_time` compile on the 32-bit `armv7-linux-androideabi` target as on the 64-bit ones). `./gradlew --no-daemon :app:testDebugUnitTest :app:assembleDebug`: green (429/429 Kotlin unit tests, unchanged; nothing under `app/` changes in 3b). | **Deviations recorded for the review pass (`REVIEW_QUEUE.md`, entry "M3.1 part 3"):** (1) the ratio-rule adaptation -- Kotlin refused an unknown compressed size and applied the per-entry ratio rules to every ZIP member; libarchive never reports a per-entry compressed size, so `compressed` is `Option<u64>` and **always `None` from the engine** (section 6.1 withdraws decision 4's "M3.2 can fill it"), the per-entry ratio rules are skipped when it is `None`, and an archive-level ratio stands in; two Kotlin test cases (`negative-compressed`, `unknown.bin` with compressed -1) therefore flip from refused to allowed in the port, each documented in `policy_tests.rs` next to its twin that the archive-level rule does refuse. (2) The link rule and its new reason string have no Kotlin counterpart. (3) Lossy names (`name_lossy`) land with 3b's `inspect`. The Kotlin `ArchiveExtractionPolicy` deletion is deferred per decision 2 to the commit that moves `ArchiveService`'s read path onto the engine (M3.4, per `DESIGN-M32-SEEKABLE-PFD.md` section 0). Ported semantics worth knowing: Kotlin's `Random(0xF71A)` stream is not reproduced bit-for-bit (the design allows this; the LCG gives the same construction rule and count). Not done here: the M3.2 design's peak-RSS measurement of `inspect` on a 200,000-entry tar belongs to M3.2a, not part 3. **3b decisions beyond the design's text, recorded here and (b, c) in `REVIEW_QUEUE.md`:** (a) `Unsupported` is detected at `archive_read_open_fd`, not at the first `archive_read_next_header` as section 6.1 says -- that is where libarchive actually runs format bidding (`_archive_read_open1` -> `choose_format`); found because the design's criterion, implemented literally at `next_header`, never fired; the classification additionally requires libarchive's own "Unrecognized archive format" message so a filter failing on a badly truncated stream stays `Fatal` (damage, not an unsupported format), which the five truncated fixtures pin. (b) `extract` decodes non-UTF-8 names lossily exactly like `inspect` (the design specifies lossy names for `inspect` only): a caller's `Selection::Paths` comes from an `Inspection`, so the two must agree or a lossy-named entry could never be selected; `entries()`/`read_entry` keep `NonUtf8Path` as the design says. (c) `Other`-kind entries (device nodes, fifos, sockets) are never offered to the provider and are counted in `ExtractReport.skipped` -- the design rules only on links, but `ArchiveEntry` carries no kind, so the provider could not decline them itself, and SAF cannot create them. (d) Sparse holes are made explicit as zeros and count against the caps (the size the file will occupy); the design is silent on sparse members. **UNVERIFIED:** no fixture reaches the `"declared"` rule (an entry whose data exceeds its own declared size): tar-family readers stop at the declared size and the ZIP reader validates its own stream, so that rule is exercised only by construction (`min(declared, max_file_bytes)` in the same code path the `"file"` rule proves). The M3.2 design's peak-RSS measurement of `inspect` on a 200,000-entry tar is M3.2a's, not done here; `max_listing_entries` ships at the designed 200,000. libarchive and its companions in the fuzz build are not sanitizer-instrumented (cmake-built C; `-Zsanitizer` reaches only the Rust code), so a C memory-safety bug surfaces only as a fault -- recorded as the design asks. `DEVICE_CHECKS.md`: nothing new (no device-visible change yet). Kotlin deletion still deferred (decision 2; M3.4). |

Against REPORT-M2's 12 MB-per-ABI core budget this is under 10% used. **c:** `./gradlew --no-daemon :app:testDebugUnitTest :app:lintDebug :app:assembleDebug` green, 467/467 (1 skipped: the ignored default-probe case); `FylzV1App.kt` is 2,287 lines after the one-line edit (`wc -l`, and `FylzV1AppSizeTest` green). Two real defects the tests found on the way and fixed: `DecoderClientTest` had never pinned a Robolectric SDK and the dropped-bind cases hit `NoSuchMethodError` for `ServiceConnection.onBindingDied` (API 26) -- pinned to `sdk = [35]` like the rest of the suite; and Robolectric's `UnregisteredInputStream` showed that a provider stream throwing a `RuntimeException` mid-copy escaped `ArchiveSource` instead of becoming `Unreadable` with the workspace deleted. The Rust gate re-run unchanged for c: `cargo test --workspace` 122/122 (+1 ignored), clippy `-D warnings`, `fmt --check`, `deny check`, `cargo +nightly fuzz build`, three-ABI `cargo ndk ... --release -p fylz-archive -p fylz-ffi-android` all green. | **a, deviations from the design, all recorded:** (1) **`is_archive_root`.** libarchive lists an ISO 9660 image's root directory as an entry named `.` (`iso9660.c`'s `build_pathname` names the nameless root that way), which the policy's Kotlin-parity `.`-segment rule refused -- every ISO image would have shown "Archive contains an unsafe path segment." in the Inspect dialog from M3.2c. `inspect` and `extract` now pass over a `Directory` entry whose path is exactly `.` or `./` (the extraction folder itself: nothing to create, nothing selectable), uncounted; entries *under* such a root (`./file`, from `tar -C dir -cf x.tar .`) are left as libarchive names them and are still refused by the `.`-segment rule -- a policy question for M3.3/M3.4, logged in `REVIEW_QUEUE.md`'s M3.2 entry. (2) The survey/design correction "7z needs a seekable descriptor for listing and data" is half right: a 7z with a *plain* header (the COPY fixture) lists through a pipe because libarchive reaches the trailing header by consuming forward; only the encoded-header shape (py7zr's default, and 7-Zip's) fails to list; data always fails ("Seek error"). Recorded, not a code change. (3) The 7z encrypted-header case **is** producible: py7zr 1.1.3 with `password=..., header_encryption=True` writes an AES-encrypted encoded header, made deterministic by patching `py7zr.compressor.get_random_bytes`; shipped as `sample-encrypted-header.7z` (not in the design's table) and pinned by `seven_zip_encrypted_header_is_unsupported`, so part 3b's `classify_header_error` path is no longer untested. (4) Fixture determinism uses `writestr` + a patched `time.time` for py7zr instead of the design's `os.utime` on source files, because py7zr records `st_ctime` (see Files). (5) `sample.iso` is 86,016 B, not the design's estimated 69,632 B (still far under 128 KiB). (6) The fuzz corpus directory convention is *not* committed files (`core/fuzz/.gitignore` lists `corpus`), so the new fixtures seed `archive_entries` through the existing `fixtures` seed-directory argument, which libFuzzer reads recursively (verified by the 150-file, 86,016 B-max seed report); the READMEs say so. (7) `max_listing_entries_stops_the_pass` uses the real 10,001-member fixture rather than the `ar` builder the design suggests (part 3b already covers the in-test archive shape with a tar). (8) ISO negative control: none, as the design anticipated; the test asserts the positive fact (the same image lists and reads through a pipe) so the record is evidence rather than a claim. **UNVERIFIED after a:** none new for the Rust side; the RSS figure is from a debug (unoptimised) host build -- allocation sizes do not depend on the profile, but bionic's allocator on device may differ from glibc's; §17 item 3 (a 100,000-entry ZIP on device) is the device-side check. **b, deviations from the design, recorded:** (9) `ArchiveEngineError` gains an `Internal { detail }` variant beyond the design's four, so `From<ArchiveError>` is total without lying: `NonUtf8Path` (which `inspect` never returns) maps to it and `DecoderService` maps it to `OUTCOME_INTERNAL`, exactly the outcome table's row for that case; without the variant the only total mapping would have called an engine bug "corrupt". (10) `total_uncompressed` sums non-directory entries only (the design does not say): an ISO directory reports its extent length as its size, which is not bytes an extraction writes. (11) `DecoderClient.call` also catches `RuntimeException` -> `Failed` (a `bindService` that throws `SecurityException`, or a service-side exception AIDL re-throws), because the client's contract is a failure value, never a thrown exception; the design lists only the timeout and `RemoteException`. (12) The two GATE-M2 review points are closed here as the design assigns: `pending` is an `AtomicReference` (compare-and-set, not merely `@Volatile`), and a `dropConnection()` racing an in-flight `ensureConnected()` is `Failed`/`null`, never a `CancellationException` -- while the caller's own cancellation still propagates (`ensureActive()`), which the design does not spell out but structured concurrency requires. Recorded under GATE-M2 in `REVIEW_QUEUE.md`. **UNVERIFIED after b:** the on-device behaviour of the new AIDL method (SELinux `isolated_app` reading the passed descriptor with libarchive's larger reads, a 100,000-entry ZIP without `TransactionTooLargeException`) is `DEVICE_CHECKS.md` section 17's, written in c; the 500-row Parcel size was measured only under Robolectric's `Parcel`. **c, deviations from the design, recorded (`REVIEW_QUEUE.md` "M3.2", items 13-18):** (13) `SizeMismatch` fires for under-delivery as well as over-delivery. (14) `ArchiveSource` has a fourth injectable, `spaceRecheckIntervalBytes` (default 64 MiB), so the mid-copy re-check is testable without copying 64 MiB through a file-backed pipe. (15) `ArchiveInspector` has an injectable `structureTimeoutMillis` (default `STRUCTURE_TIMEOUT_MILLIS`) for the same reason. (16) `ArchiveInspectionResult.summaryOrNull` (a sealed-class member) is what `FylzV1App.kt:1012` reads, so the Extract action's line needs no new import and the file stays on its ratchet; the design's `Ready.summary.hasEncryptedEntries` is what it evaluates to. (17) `ArchiveInspectionResult.failureMessage()` centralises the user-facing sentences the design assigns per result ("took too long to read", "could not be read safely", per-outcome for `Refused`, the cause's message for `SourceFailed`) so the overlay and the preview say the same thing. (18) A provider stream's `RuntimeException` mid-copy is `Unreadable` (see Verification). Also new here and not in the design: the dialog's dismiss button reads "Close" for a non-ZIP family (there is nothing to cancel), a lossy-names note in both the dialog and the preview, and a "Links" metric in the preview when `linkCount > 0`. **UNVERIFIED, all device-only (`DEVICE_CHECKS.md` section 17):** the default `statSize >= 0` probe on a genuine pipe (Robolectric's is file-backed -- the one `@Ignore`d test); SELinux `isolated_app` reading the passed descriptor under libarchive's larger, seeking reads; a 100,000-entry ZIP without `TransactionTooLargeException`; the real kill/hang behaviour of the application-scoped binding; that Google Drive (or another streaming provider) actually hands back a pipe so item 2's "copied to temporary storage" line appears. `FylzCore.listArchive`/`extractEntryAt`. `DecoderClient` rewritten for concurrency (section 2.2/2.9): per-binding `Binding : ServiceConnection` carrying a **generation**, `dropConnection(generation)` a no-op for any other binding, an **in-flight counter**, the idle timer (60 s, `dropIfIdle` also checks in-flight == 0) armed only at zero and cancelled at every call start, a dedicated 4-thread daemon pool for transactions and drains, **retry once** for a transaction whose `RemoteException` arrived after another call's drop bumped the generation (a bind dropped while connecting stays `Failed`, as M3.2b's test requires); `callStreaming(archive, inactivityMillis, drain, block)`: the client creates the pipe, the drain takes its pool thread before the transaction starts (no reader-less transaction can hold a thread), `block` gets the write end and the client closes its own copy in the transaction's `finally`, `DrainStream` applies the Robolectric rule (`-1` is final only once the transaction has returned AND one further read also returns `-1`; otherwise sleep 10 ms and retry), liveness = drained bytes OR `offsetProbe(archive)` (`Os.lseek(fd, 0, SEEK_CUR)`; injectable, `null` under Robolectric) sampled every `livenessPollMillis`, abandon closes the read end (waking a blocked drain), cancels the drain, abandons the transaction and drops the connection; a failing drain closes both pipe ends (the engine's write gets EPIPE) and is **thrown** to the caller with the connection kept. `FylzApplication`: one `decoderClient`, `archiveCacheSweeper`, `archiveEntryCache`, `archiveCatalog`. **Tests (Kotlin, new: `ArchiveDocumentIdTest` 6, `ArchiveListingCodecTest` 14, `ArchiveTreeTest` 9, `ArchiveCacheSweeperTest` 4, `ArchiveCatalogTest` 14, `ArchiveEntryCacheTest` 11 incl. the amendment-3 interleave case (`FakeArchiveDecoder` reads the archive through the descriptor it is handed, positioning it before every 64-byte chunk with a sleep in between -- under Robolectric an in-process stub receives the very same descriptor object, so a shared one would corrupt), `DecoderClientStreamingTest` 12 (a sibling of `DecoderClientTest`), `DecoderServiceMappingTest` +4, `ArchiveParcelablesTest` +2; New `actions/BrowserStateBuilder.kt`: `BrowserStateInputs` (18 named inputs) and `buildBrowserState`, the assembly that left `FylzV1App.kt`, deriving `hasActiveTab`, `canNavigateUp`, `currentFolderIsFavourite` and `locationKind` as the app did. `BuiltInActions`: one named predicate `WRITABLE_LOCATION` (`locationKind != ARCHIVE`) on exactly the design's thirteen ids -- `cut`, `move-to`, `recycle`, `rename`, `rename.batch`, `tags`, `paste`, `new-folder`, `new-file`, `scan-to-pdf`, `favourite.toggle`, `ai.organize`, `find-duplicates`; `copy`, `copy-to`, `share`, `compress`, `pdf.tools`, `select.*`, `open`, `open-with`, sort/view, `navigate.up`, `refresh`, `extract` unchanged; the `open`/`open-with` double-tap split uses `isBrowsableArchive`, so a double-tap browses an archive. `FylzV1App.kt`: `openEntry` pushes `(ArchiveDocumentsProvider.rootUri(entry), entry.name)` for a browsable archive inside `runCatching` (the depth refusal toasts through the existing channel) and `(entry.uri, entry.name)` for a directory inside one; the search effect is off while the current location is an archive; `refresh()` calls `archiveCatalog.forgetFailures()` so pull-to-refresh retries a memoised failure; the `BrowserState` construction is `buildBrowserState(BrowserStateInputs(...))` with the same `remember` keys. `EntryThumbnail.thumbnailable` is false for archive Uris (no thumbnails inside archives). `DestinationChooserSheet`: `destinationTabs(tabs)` drops tabs whose current location is an archive. `PreviewPane`: the archive gate is `ZIP_CONTAINER_EXTENSIONS` or `BrowsableArchiveFormats.matches(name)`; `ZipArchivePreview` is `ArchivePreview`, loading `ArchivePreviewState` (summary + tree, first 500 root children) through `ArchiveCatalog`, showing "N entries with unsafe paths are hidden." and "Damaged after N entries; showing what could be read" when present; `SEMANTIC_ZIP_DOCUMENTS` keep `ZipDocumentPreview`; an entry previews as any document Uri. `FileFormatRegistry.archives` gains `lz4 tzst tar.lz4 cb7 warc` (and the `tar.lz4` compound) so `EntryKind.ARCHIVE` agrees with the browsable set. Tests (new): `BrowsableArchiveFormatsTest` 3, `BrowserStateBuilderTest` 4, `DestinationChooserSheetTest` 1, `browse/SessionRestoreArchiveLocationTest` 2 (a restored three-deep archive location lists through the hosted provider with the fake decoder, once, and Up lists the archive root from the same tree; a vanished source fails with the message and the location survives the decode); `ActionResolverGoldenTest`: `LegacyOracle.writableLocation` as the named rule, the thirteen expected rows take it, `BrowserStateFixtures` gains `archiveRootNoSelection`, `archiveFolderWithSelection`, `archiveWithClipboard`; `NoHardCodedMenusTest`, `ShortcutTableTest` untouched. **Ratchet:** `FylzV1App.kt` 2287 -> 2271 lines (`wc -l`), `fylzV1AppMaxLines = 2271`. **Gate (c):** `./gradlew --no-daemon :app:testDebugUnitTest :app:lintDebug :app:assembleDebug` green -- 569 tests, 0 failed, 1 skipped (pre-existing); no Rust touched, so the `core` gate and the three-ABI build stand from a. **Deviations from the design, recorded (also `REVIEW_QUEUE.md` "M3.3" items 22-45):** (1) the codec's `path` is the engine's string (lossy-decoded, `NAME_LOSSY` set) rather than the undecoded raw bytes, because Java's and Rust's replacement rules differ and `extract_entry_at`'s byte-exact match must see what the listing carried; (2) ordinals are raw header indices per amendment 1, on `EntryMetadata`, so the ISO/`./`-rooted tar's first entry is ordinal 1; (3) eight golden `.fzl` files, not one; (4) `structural_refusal` on `Inspection`, the record, the Parcelable and the sidecar (amendment 4); (5) the `.`-segment rule is a deliberate deviation from Kotlin parity (amendment 5; owner review); (6) SIGPIPE ignored in the FFI crate (amendment 2); (7) the source, not a descriptor, is pinned (`PinnedSource`; amendment 3), the design's "pins one `ArchiveSource.Resolved`" being the bug it fixes; (8) the summary sidecar `<key>.summary.json`, which the design did not have (a disk-first load had no policy verdict to refuse entries with); (9) `ArchiveExtractResult` is the minimal `outcome/message/bytesWritten` (M3.4 extends it); (10) retry-once is scoped to transactions whose binding another call dropped, and `drain` may therefore run twice; (11) a drain failure is thrown, not `Failed`; (12) `Failure.TooDeep`/`NotAnEntry` beyond the design's four; (13) reference-counted handles instead of "close on eviction", so an in-flight fill is never cut off; (14) directories and implicit entries are refused with "Folders cannot be opened as files."; (15) the sweep runs from `ArchiveCatalog.open` and `ArchiveInspector.inspect` (first wins); (16) `tarfile` writes a directory member as `dir` and libarchive's tar reader reports it as `dir/`, so the trailing-slash case rides on that and on `sample-cd.zip`; (17) libarchive's ZIP reader already rewrites `\` to `/` (`backslash.zip` lists as `dir/file.txt`), so the tree's rule is exercised on a hand-built listing; (18) `sample-entries.zip`'s TTF is a stub; (19) `rows_truncated` is `false` from `archive_list_into`; (20) `ArchiveInspector` gains a `sweeper` parameter. **b:** (21) the attach test builds the `ProviderInfo` from the manifest XML when `resolveContentProvider` returns null (Robolectric without `includeAndroidResources`) -- the same attributes from the same file, and no intent filter asserted; (22) `getDocumentType` is implemented so `resolver.getType` (share, Open with) answers without a full row query; (23) a small `FALLBACK_MIME` table behind `MimeTypeMap`; (24) the root row's name is the source document's `DISPLAY_NAME` from its own provider, falling back to the id-derived name; (25) `openDocument` honours `CancellationSignal` by cancelling the fill and throwing `OperationCanceledException`; (26) `DocNode.children` also turns `EXTRA_ERROR` into an `IOException` (the design named only `listChildren`), so a directory copy-out of a failed archive fails loudly instead of copying an empty folder; (27) a stale ordinal in an id is "not found", never a different entry. **c:** (28) the dead `private fun fileIcon` in `FylzV1App.kt` (unreferenced since the entry row took `EntryThumbnail`) and its icon imports were removed together with the `BrowserState` extraction, which is where the 16-line saving comes from (the design estimated about 10); (29) `FileFormatRegistry.archives` was extended (`lz4 tzst tar.lz4 cb7 warc`) although the design said it "stays private" -- it stays private, but without the additions a `.cb7` classified as `OTHER` while browsing as an archive; (30) `ArchivePreview` loads through `ArchiveCatalog` (one listing on disk shared with the folder view, and the tree gives the quarantined count and the partial message) rather than through `ArchiveInspector.inspect`, so the preview no longer shows `rowsTruncated` from the engine but caps at 500 root children itself; (31) `refresh()` forgets memoised catalog failures (the design left the retry path implicit); (32) the restore-and-list case is a sibling class `SessionRestoreArchiveLocationTest` rather than a `SessionCodecTest` case, because it needs the hosted providers; (33) `LocationKind.of(uri)` is derived from the Uri's authority at build time -- `FolderLocation` carries no kind field; (34) `EntryThumbnail` refuses archive Uris (REVIEW_QUEUE item 11 made concrete). **d (docs):** `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` gains "Archives as documents (M3.3)" (provider contract, ids and ordinals, the pipe transport and its inactivity/liveness rule, catalog keys and single-flight, the tree's rules, entry materialisation and refusals, budgets and sweeping, the 60 s idle unbind) and its decoder section no longer says "no idle unbind yet"; `DEVICE_CHECKS.md` section 18 "M3.3 -- archive browsing" carries the design's eleven section-2.11 items with commands and expected results; `REVIEW_QUEUE.md`'s M3.3 entry (items 1-45) is final and M3.2 items 11 and 12 are marked closed. **UNVERIFIED (all device-only, `DEVICE_CHECKS.md` section 18):** the Compose paths themselves -- tap and double-tap into an archive, breadcrumb `Downloads / photos.zip / 2024`, Up out of an archive, the disabled actions greyed in the menus, `ArchivePreview` and an entry previewed through its document Uri, search staying off -- since the app has no Compose test harness (the registry rows, the builder, the format set, the destination filter and the restore path are unit-tested);  the manifest's `<provider>` as the platform reads it (exported, `MANAGE_DOCUMENTS`, Uri grants to another app through share/Open with), `TransactionTooLargeException` under `ACTION_SEND_MULTIPLE` of thousands of ~250-character Uris (REVIEW_QUEUE item 19); real pipe EOF/backpressure semantics (Robolectric's pipes are file-backed), `Os.lseek` liveness on a Binder dup's shared offset (the probe degrades to bytes-only here), SELinux `isolated_app` writing the passed pipe and reading the staged/materialised files (section 2.2), the AndroidRuntime SIGPIPE disposition claim, and that the interleave the amendment-3 test provokes under Robolectric's shared in-process descriptor matches a device's dup semantics. |

REVIEW_QUEUE `M3.4` items 1-36):** **M3.3 contract verified in code first, no gap** (REVIEW_QUEUE M3.4 item 25): (i) `EntryMetadata.ordinal` from the one counter `Reader.headers_read`, incremented in `next_header` before the root `continue`, shared by listing, `extract`, `extract_entry_at` and now `extract_blocks`; (ii) `ignore_sigpipe()` (`Once`) at the start of every exported archive function; (iii) `PinnedSource.open()` per `:decoders` call, the entry cache re-opening per fill; (iv) `<key>.summary.json` with `structuralRefusal`, `readCached` failing closed, Rust's `structural_only()` verdict; (v) `.` segments and a leading `./` are no-ops in `validate_path`/`normalized_path_key`, `..` refused. **Rust `fylz-archive`:** `Selection::Ranges(Vec<(u32, u32)>)` (inclusive ordinal ranges, sorted and merged, a pass stops reading after the highest one); `trait BlockSink { begin(&EntryMetadata) -> Result<bool>; write(ordinal, &[u8]); end(ordinal, bytes, Option<Warning>); failed(ordinal, FailKind, msg) }`, `enum Warning { Other(String) }`, `enum FailKind { Crc, Size, Decode, Other }`; `extract_blocks(fd, &Selection, &ExtractLimits, &mut dyn BlockSink) -> Result<ExtractReport>` and `extract_blocks_cancellable(.., cancel: &mut dyn FnMut() -> bool) -> BlocksOutcome { report, stop_ordinal: Option<u32>, result }`, the cancel hook polled after every `CANCEL_POLL_HEADERS = 64` headers in both passes; `inspect_for_extraction(fd, &Selection, cancel) -> SelectedEntries { archive_bytes, entries }` (the header pass gathering the selection's metadata, early exit, a `FAILED` header passed over); `extract()` is now an adapter (a `ProviderSink` that declines links/`Other` on the provider's behalf and turns the first per-entry failure into `Err(Failed)`), `extract_entry_at` the one-range case (an `EntrySink` with the byte-exact path check; links and directories still yield 0 bytes); `Reader::check` split into `check_header`/`check_data` (`ARCHIVE_FAILED = -25` -> `ArchiveError::Failed`, `RETRY`/`FATAL` -> `Fatal`; `check` keeps mapping everything to `Fatal` for the open path, lookups and listing), `next_header_raw` counts a `FAILED` header's ordinal (libarchive consumed it) and returns `Failed`, `next_header` maps it to `Fatal` (M3.2's listing mapping kept: `damaged-after-3.tar` still lists partially); `stream_current_entry_data` generalised over a writer closure (zeros for sparse holes included) and returning `DataEnd::Complete { written, warning } | Failed { kind, message }`: a data `ARCHIVE_FAILED` is classified by message ("CRC" -> `Crc`, "wrong size" -> `Size`, else `Decode`), an `ARCHIVE_WARN` whose message names a CRC is `Failed(Crc)` with the flagged block withheld (7-Zip's mismatch, swallowed before), any other `WARN` keeps the block and ends with `Warning::Other`; `ArchiveError::Failed(String)` and `ArchiveError::Cancelled`; `ExtractReport.entries_failed`; `ExtractLimits.max_path_depth`/`max_name_length` for the per-entry structural re-check (`policy::structural_check`, run **after** the sink accepted the entry, no data read on failure; REVIEW_QUEUE item 29). `policy.rs`: `evaluate_selection(archive_bytes, &selected, &limits)` (archive size, entry count with `>`, duplicate keys, unknown size, per-file, running total with overflow guard, per-entry ratio when a compressed size is known, the archive-level ratio of the **selected** bytes; no path/link rule -- item 35) and `pub(crate) structural_check`. **Rust `fylz-ffi-android`:** new `src/frames.rs` (the `FZX1` writer: `BEGIN 0x01 ordinal:u32 declared:i64 kind:u8 path_len:u32 path`, `DATA 0x02 ordinal len:u32<=1 MiB bytes` (larger blocks split), `END 0x03 ordinal bytes:u64 warn:u8 msg_len:u16 msg`, `FAIL 0x04 ordinal kind:u8 msg_len msg`, `DONE 0x05 entries:u32 bytes:u64 failed:u32`, `ABORT 0x06 msg_len msg`; magic written with the first frame; `BrokenPipe` -> `Cancelled`; an over-long path is a stand-alone `FAIL`); `archive_extract_ranges(fd, ranges: Vec<ArchiveOrdinalRangeRecord { first, last }>, limits, sink_fd) -> ArchiveExtractRecord { outcome: ArchiveExtractOutcomeRecord (Ok, Refused, NotSeekable, Unsupported, Corrupt, LimitExceeded, Cancelled, Internal), message, entries_written, bytes_written, entries_failed, stop_ordinal }` -- header pass, `evaluate_selection` (a refusal writes no frame), extraction pass writing frames, `MAGIC ABORT` for a fatal at any point (the reader requires the magic with `Corrupt`), a record rather than an exception so counts survive a fatal; while writing these tests, all fixed in this same commit (REVIEW_QUEUE `M3.5` items 18-25; see that file for full detail):** `Walker.addFile` spooled every unknown-size entry regardless of format (now tar-only); `CompressProblem.ArchiveRefused` was declared but never constructed (a bad source archive refused the whole plan instead of being a skippable per-source problem); `resolveConflict`'s `existingParts` included the base name itself; `queryDisplayName` used the classic query overload `DocNode.load` already documents as unsupported here; `ArchiveCreator` never cleaned up a previous attempt's staged output before a resumed run re-staged from scratch; the engine's own `PROTOCOL_ERROR` outcome could never be recognised by `writeFailureCode` (only the message text was checked, never the outcome code -- a new `EngineOutcomeException` carries it); **most significantly, `DecoderClient.callTwoPipes` never closed its own copies of `inRead`/`outWrite` once the transaction finished** -- unlike `callStreaming`'s own proven `DrainStream`-wrapped pattern -- which would leave this process's own copy of the engine's output pipe open for the whole drain phase and `drain()`'s real EOF would never arrive **on a real device**, not merely a Robolectric-testing artifact (confirmed with a plain-JVM `RobolectricPipeReadTest` proving a real OS pipe's read end never sees EOF while any write end anywhere stays open); fixed by closing both in the transaction's own `finally` and reusing `callStreaming`'s `DrainStream` retry-until-done wrapper for the drain too; and `ArchiveCreator.feedFile` could call `ArchiveFrameWriter.abort()` with an entry still open on the wire (a source failing between `entry()` and `end()`), which the engine correctly rejects as a protocol violation, masking the real failure -- fixed by ending the entry with whatever partial bytes arrived first. **Gate (b):** Kotlin unit tests 654 -> 713 (+59; 1 pre-existing skip unrelated to this milestone), lint and assembleDebug green; Rust workspace unchanged at 202 (b touches no Rust), clippy/fmt/deny/fuzz-build green, three-ABI `cargo ndk --release` green. Line counts: `FylzV1App.kt` 2267 -> **2270** (at the ratchet exactly, zero headroom left); `FylzAppShell.kt` untouched at 100 (ratchet 101). | **Architecture choice (7z-scope narrowing and the two open questions the brief posed):** ZIP-family only, per M3.5's own scope (7z has no writer in this codebase) -- logged in `REVIEW_QUEUE.md` M3.6. Reading existing entries reuses the archive-sourced-entry Uri pattern `CompressPlanner` already has (no native copy-through added to the engine): each retained/renamed old entry becomes a manifest row whose source is a Uri through `ArchiveDocumentsProvider`, read exactly the way `ArchiveEntryCache`/`extract_entry_at` already stream it -- zero new Rust code. The new operation type question was resolved as "model an edit as create-from-mixed-manifest plus one extra finalize step", not a new `FileOperationType.ARCHIVE_EDIT`: `CompressPlan.replaceOriginalUri` plus `ArchiveCreator.replaceOriginal()` is the entire extra surface, reusing every bit of `ARCHIVE`'s existing queue/journal/retry/WorkManager plumbing that a new type would have had to duplicate; the round-trip test exercises this through the real, hosted provider and passed on the first architecturally-correct attempt (only test-harness bugs needed fixing, see below). Deviations found and fixed while testing (also in `REVIEW_QUEUE.md` M3.6): (1) `finalizeParts()` had to become `suspend` for the replace path's provider calls; (2) the v4->v5 migration guard must be `oldVersion == 4`, not `< 5`, or the ALTER TABLE double-adds a column `createCompressTables` already creates fresh; (3) `.fylz-trash` must be created explicitly under the destination before calling `replaceWithRecycleFallback`, since that helper's own contract ("use `.fylz-trash` if present, else delete") is a best-effort fallback, not the unconditional recycle M3.6 requires; (4) the test helper's archive Uri had to be built rooted at the volume (`buildDocumentUriUsingTree(treeUri, docId)`), not self-rooted at the document, or Android's own `enforceTree` check refuses the read-back after `RecycleBinService` renames the document out from under a self-rooted tree -- a test-only bug, confirmed not a production issue since production Uris are always volume-rooted. **UNVERIFIED (device-only, `DEVICE_CHECKS.md` section 21):** the Compose paths themselves (Rename/Recycle re-enabled inside a browsed ZIP entry, the "Add entries" Tools-menu item and its file picker, the confirm/cancel dialogs) since the app has no Compose test harness; a real device's SAF behaviour recycling a multi-GB archive; real timing of `.fylz-trash` creation racing a concurrent operation on the same archive. |
| **MC.0** | done, verified | `ed9e61b`, `432c552`, `71e6b84`, `d3c3e9d`, `959e696`, (this commit) | New `app/src/main/java/io/github/mbaliga/fylz/actions/`: `ActionId` (+ grammar `parse()`), `IconRef`, `ConfirmPolicy`, `Origin`, `ActionBody`, `TargetKind`, `GestureId`, `Surfaces.kt` (`Bar`/`MenuId`/`RoomId`), `KeyChord`, `ActionTarget`, `Placement`, `ActionDef`, `BuiltInBinding`, `BrowserState`, `ActionContext`, `ActionRegistry` (+ `RegistryProblem`), `ActionResolver` (+ `ResolvedAction`/`PlacementQuery`), `ActionDispatcher`, `BuiltInActions` (the full built-in table, design §2.5: selection bar, top app bar/overflow, browser row/sort menu, rows/cards, Locations/Library rail/Tools/Recovery rooms, the Archive Tools menu, the three edge-room-open gestures — 57 built-ins total), `actions/legacy/LegacyAvailability.kt` (every availability expression named in design §2.7 item 1, lifted word for word). New `core/crates/fylz-actions/{Cargo.toml,src/lib.rs}` (plain `ActionId`/`ActionDef`/`Placement`/`ConfirmPolicy`/`ActionBody`/`Origin`/`KeyChord`/`GestureId`/`TargetKind`/`IconRef`/`Bar`/`MenuId`/`RoomId`, no serde, no dependencies), added to `core/Cargo.toml`'s workspace members, `core/Cargo.lock` updated. Changed `app/src/main/java/io/github/mbaliga/fylz/ui/FylzV1App.kt`: every inline availability expression the design names (`canRename`/`canExtract`/`canPdfTools`, the five overflow `enabled=` expressions, the clipboard chip's, navigate-up's, select-all's, the rail's Favourite's) now calls the matching `LegacyAvailability` function instead — same code path, same values; a `BrowserState`, an `ActionContext` implementation and an `ActionRegistry` are now constructed inside `FylzV1Workspace` (the `ActionContext` closes over the same locals/named functions the old lambdas already use, not `remember`-wrapped itself since its captures are plain per-recomposition locals — a keyless `remember` would freeze them stale; `BrowserState` and the registry are `remember`ed correctly, the latter with no keys since it is pure static data); gained an `onShowHistory`/`operationsNeedingAttention` parameter pair threaded from `FylzAppShell` (mirroring the existing `recoveryRoom`/`overlays` pattern) so the registry's Recovery-room and count-carrying label have something real to read even though nothing dispatches through them yet. Changed `FylzAppShell.kt` to compute and pass those two. No renderer reads the registry yet and no old menu, button, gesture or shortcut was rewired — this commit is the types, the registry/resolver/dispatcher, and the differential oracle, exactly design §2.8 item 1's scope.  **MC.0b** (design §2.8 item 2): new `app/src/main/java/io/github/mbaliga/fylz/ui/actions/` package -- `ActionButton.kt` (moved verbatim out of `ui/FylzV1App.kt`), `BuiltinIcons.kt` (a `name -> ImageVector` map for the 12 `IconRef.Builtin` names the selection bar's built-ins use), `SelectionActionBarRenderer.kt` (`SelectionActionBar(resolver, dispatcher, state, ctx)`: resolves `PlacementQuery.SelectionBar`, draws each item through `ActionButton` with the resolved `enabled`/`label`/icon, dispatches on click, keeps the trailing `fylz.select.clear` as the plain `TextButton("Clear")` it always was). Changed `ui/FylzV1App.kt`: `Scaffold`'s `bottomBar` now reads `if (selectedEntries.isNotEmpty()) { SelectionActionBar(actionResolver, actionDispatcher, browserState, actionContext) }`, replacing the old private `SelectionActionBar` composable's 13-lambda call site (every lambda there duplicated logic `actionContext` -- built in MC.0a -- already had); `actionResolver`/`actionDispatcher` are now `remember(actionRegistry)`ed alongside it. Deleted the old private `SelectionActionBar` and `ActionButton` composables from `ui/FylzV1App.kt` (109 lines net). Six now-unused icon imports removed (`FolderCopy`, `DriveFileMove`, `Delete`, `Edit`, `Tag`, `Share` -- each had exactly one call site, inside the deleted composable; confirmed via grep before removal, `fileIcon()`'s own Archive/PictureAsPdf/TextSnippet uses are untouched).  **MC.0c** (design §2.8 item 3, with the command palette moved up from MC.0e per this task's instructions -- `fylz.commands` renders in this commit's overflow menu, so it must not open a dead item): new `ui/actions/TopAppBarRenderer.kt` (`TopAppBarActions`: the clipboard chip -- `fylz.paste`, trailing `fylz.clipboard.clear` -- then `fylz.view.toggle`/`fylz.refresh` as `IconButton`s, then the one `DropdownMenu` over `Menu(OVERFLOW)`; the chip's Cut/Copy leading icon and the view toggle's List/GridView icon stay read off `BrowserState.clipboard`/`viewMode` directly, since a single static `IconRef` can't represent a state-dependent icon -- same as the old code chose). New `ui/actions/BrowserRowRenderer.kt` (`NavigateUpButton`/`SelectAllButton`/`SortMenuButton`: `Toolbar(BROWSER_ROW)`'s two icons plus the sort `DropdownMenu` over `Menu(SORT)`, with the same checked marks, direction arrows and re-click-flips-direction behaviour, via `ctx.setSortField`/`toggleFoldersFirst`). New `ui/actions/CommandPaletteDialog.kt` (a `Dialog` with a text field filtering `ActionResolver.paletteCandidates` by label; Enter or a click dispatches and closes). Extended `ui/actions/BuiltinIcons.kt` with the 7 more icon names the top app bar/overflow/browser row need (`Close`, `Refresh`, `CreateNewFolder`, `ArrowBack`, `SelectAll`, `GridView`, `List`). Changed `actions/BuiltInBinding.kt` (new `paletteVisible: Boolean = true` field), `actions/ActionResolver.kt` (new `paletteCandidates(state)`: every binding with `requiresTarget == null && paletteVisible`, then `visibleWhen && enabledWhen`, sorted by label), `actions/ActionContext.kt` (new `openCommandPalette()`), `actions/BuiltInActions.kt` (`fylz.commands`'s `run` now calls `ctx.openCommandPalette()`; the four self-contained Recovery overlay cards -- `fylz.history.files`/`.backup.plans`/`.backup.import`/`.archive.tools` -- gained `paletteVisible = false`). Changed `ui/FylzV1App.kt`: the top app bar's `actions = {}` lambda, `FileBrowser`'s navigate-up/sort/select-all controls, and the old `SortMenu` composable are all deleted and replaced by calls into the three new renderers; a `commandPaletteOpen` dialog flag (replacing the old `moreExpanded`, now owned by `TopAppBarRenderer` itself) opens `CommandPaletteDialog` alongside the app's other dialogs. Fourteen now-fully-unused imports removed (`DropdownMenu`, `DropdownMenuItem`, `InputChip`, `ArrowUpward`, `ArrowDownward`, `ContentCut`, `ContentCopy`, `CreateNewFolder`, `GridView`, `List`, `MoreVert`, `Refresh`, `SelectAll`, `Sort`, `SortDirection` -- each confirmed by grep to have zero remaining call sites; `PictureAsPdf`/`TextSnippet`/`Close`/`LegacyAvailability` kept, since `fileIcon()`/`OperationProgressRow`/the rail's Favourite still use them). New test-only `app/src/test/java/io/github/mbaliga/fylz/actions/NoHardCodedMenusTest.kt` (walks `app/src/main/java`, climbing from `user.dir` to find it so it survives whatever directory Gradle happens to run the test JVM from; fails on a word-boundary-matched `DropdownMenuItem(`/`ActionButton(` outside `ui/actions/` -- word-boundary matched so `FloatingActionButton(` in the Recovery overlays is never mistaken for `ActionButton(`).  **MC.0d** (design §2.8 item 4): new `ui/actions/RoomActionsRenderer.kt` -- `LibraryRailRoom` (replaces `LibraryRail`: the rail's three buttons in resolved order/enabled/label, favourite's icon read off the resolved `checked` the same way the top app bar's chip already does, the display-only favourites list kept verbatim), `LocationsRoom` (replaces the old private composable: the `WordWheelRail` tab list is untouched -- places, not commands -- its "add" and "close" affordances now dispatch `fylz.tab.add`/`fylz.tab.close`, the latter with `ActionTarget.Tab(tab.id)` for the row it is drawn against), `ToolsRoom` (replaces `ToolsRoom` and deletes the `ToolsAction` enum: five `ToolsRow`s plus three theme rows, all resolved from `Room(TOOLS)`, in order; every row stays text-only, exactly as `ToolsRow` always drew it -- the Tools room's own icon names are declared but never looked up here), `RecoveryRoom` (replaces `FylzAppShell.kt`'s five hard-coded `RecoveryActionCard`s: one card per resolved `Room(RECOVERY)` action, `fylz.history.operations`'s FAB dispatching to `ctx.showOperationHistory()` -> `onShowHistory()`, the other four placing their existing self-contained overlay composable from a small `Map<ActionId, @Composable (ActionResolver, BrowserState) -> Unit>` table), and the moved `ToolsRow`/`RecoveryActionCard`/`RoomHeading` composables. New `ui/actions/ArchiveToolsMenuRenderer.kt` (`ArchiveToolsMenuDialog(resolver, state, busy, onDismiss, onSelect: (ActionId) -> Unit)`: renders `Menu(ARCHIVE_TOOLS)`'s two buttons in resolved order/label/enabled; a click never dispatches through `ActionDispatcher` -- both bindings' `run` stay documented no-ops -- so the renderer hands the clicked id to `onSelect`, which `ArchiveToolsOverlay` maps to its own two existing picker launchers, keeping all of the overlay's state exactly where it was). Extended `ui/actions/BuiltinIcons.kt` with the 11 names the rail/Recovery/Archive-Tools menu need (`Star`, `StarBorder`, `RestoreFromTrash`, `Cloud`, `Build`, `History`, `Backup`, `RestorePage`, `Unarchive`, `Warning`, plus reusing the existing `Archive`); the Tools room's own names are declared in `BuiltInActions.kt` but never resolved to a vector since its rows are text-only. Changed `actions/BuiltInActions.kt`: `fylz.protect`'s icon `"Lock"` -> `"Archive"` (the old Create-ZIP button actually drew `Icons.Outlined.Archive`, never a lock) and the three `themeAction` rows' icon `"Brightness"` -> `"None"` (the old theme rows drew no icon at all, a filled/hollow square marker, not an `Icon` -- both were MC.0a names with no real vector, flagged in this task's brief). Changed `ui/ArchiveToolsOverlay.kt`: takes a new `resolver`/`state` pair, its old 44-line hard-coded two-button `AlertDialog` replaced by one call into `ArchiveToolsMenuDialog`. Changed `ui/FylzV1App.kt`: deleted `LibraryRail`, the old private `LocationsRoom`, the `ToolsAction` enum, the old private `ToolsRoom`, `RoomHeading`, `ToolsRow`, `ThemeMode.readableLabel()` and the `HOME_WHEEL_ID`/`ADD_WHEEL_ID` consts (all moved or replaced); the `SpatialShell` `left`/`right`/`bottom` slots and the wide-gated rail call site now call the four new renderers; `FylzV1App`/`FylzV1Workspace` lost the `recoveryRoom: @Composable () -> Unit` parameter entirely, since the Recovery room is now built inline from the registry the same way Locations/Tools already are, rather than handed down as a slot. Nineteen now-fully-unused imports removed from `FylzV1App.kt` (`FolderOpen`, `Star`, `StarBorder`, `RestoreFromTrash`, `WordWheelRail`, `WheelItem`, `FilledTonalButton`, `OutlinedButton`, `FontWeight`, `rememberScrollState`, `verticalScroll`, `clickable`, `statusBarsPadding`, each confirmed by grep to have zero remaining call sites once the composables that used them moved out; `Archive`/`PictureAsPdf`/`ViewSidebar`/`LegacyAvailability` kept, since `fileIcon()`/the browser-state construction still use them). Changed `ui/FylzAppShell.kt`: deleted the private `RecoveryHome` and `RecoveryActionCard` composables and the `recoveryRoom = { ... }` argument to `FylzV1App`; twelve now-unused imports removed (every Compose layout/Material3/icon import `RecoveryHome`/`RecoveryActionCard` alone had used -- the surviving `FylzAppShell` composable draws no UI of its own any more, only the operations journal, `showHistory` state and `OperationHistoryDialog`). Extended `app/src/test/java/io/github/mbaliga/fylz/actions/NoHardCodedMenusTest.kt`'s forbidden pattern list with `ToolsRow(`/`RecoveryActionCard(`, word-boundary matched like the existing two.  **MC.0e** (design §2.8 item 5): `actions/BrowserState.kt` gained `activeTabId: String? = null` and `registryProblemCount: Int = 0` (design §2.3 clarification), both defaulted so `BrowserStateFixtures.kt`'s named-arg `base()` and the golden test stay byte-identical; `FylzV1Workspace` now supplies `activeTabId = activeTabId` and `registryProblemCount = actionRegistry.problems.size` when it assembles the state. `actions/BuiltInActions.kt`: `fylz.customisation.problems`'s `visibleWhen` is now `{ it.registryProblemCount > 0 }` (was `{ false }`) and its `run` calls the new `ctx.showRegistryProblems()`. New `actions/KeyRouter.kt` (pure Kotlin): `Routed` (a sealed `Dispatch(id, target)`/`ClearFocus`) and `KeyRouter.route(chord, textFieldFocused, registry, state): Routed?` -- a focused text field routes every chord to `null` except Escape (`ClearFocus`); otherwise a `registry.shortcuts()` lookup gated by `visibleWhen`/`enabledWhen`, with Enter's target resolved to `state.focused` and Ctrl+W's to `state.activeTabId`, never guessed. Changed `actions/ActionDispatcher.kt`: new `gesture(gesture: GestureId, target: ActionTarget?, state, ctx)` -- finds the first binding whose `Placement.Gesture(gesture, targetWhen)` accepts `target` (or has no `targetWhen`), gated the same way `run` gates a click. Changed `actions/ActionContext.kt`: new `showRegistryProblems()`. New `ui/actions/RegistryProblemsDialog.kt` (a minimal `AlertDialog` listing `registry.problems`, one line each). Changed `ui/actions/CommandPaletteDialog.kt`: new `onFocusChanged: (Boolean) -> Unit = {}` param wired onto its own text field, reporting into the workspace's shared text-field-focused flag alongside the search field's, per design's own text (moot in practice since a `Dialog` opens its own Android window the root's `onKeyEvent` never sees anyway -- cheap defence in depth, not load-bearing). Changed `ui/FylzV1App.kt`: `FileRowV1`/`FileCard`/`SearchResults` traded their `onOpen`/`onOpenExternal`/`onToggleSelection` callback params for `dispatcher`/`state`/`ctx` -- `combinedClickable`'s tap/double-tap/long-press now call `dispatcher.gesture(ITEM_TAP/ITEM_DOUBLE_TAP/ITEM_LONG_PRESS, ActionTarget.Entry(entry), state, ctx)` and the checkbox dispatches `fylz.select.toggle` by id directly; `FileBrowser` lost those same three params (it already carried `dispatcher`/`state`/`ctx`) and gained `onSearchFocusChanged: (Boolean) -> Unit`, wired onto the search field's own `Modifier.onFocusChanged`. `FylzV1Workspace` gained `textFieldFocused` state, a root `FocusRequester` + `LocalFocusManager` + `LocalWindowInfo.current.isWindowFocused`, and a `LaunchedEffect(windowFocused, textFieldFocused)` that requests root focus whenever the window is focused and no text field is -- covering first composition, the search field losing focus, and any dialog (its own Android window) closing, in one check rather than enumerating every dialog's own dismiss handler. The `SpatialShell` `modifier` gained `.focusRequester(root).focusable().onKeyEvent { ... }` (not `onPreviewKeyEvent`, which would run *before* a focused child like the search field and steal its own Delete/Ctrl+V/Ctrl+A): builds a `KeyChord` from the event's `key`/`isCtrlPressed`/`isShiftPressed`/`isAltPressed`/`isMetaPressed`, routes it through `KeyRouter.route`, and either dispatches or calls `focusManager.clearFocus()`. `ShakeToRefresh`'s `onShake` now calls `actionDispatcher.gesture(GestureId.SHAKE, null, browserState, actionContext)` instead of `refresh()` directly. The `SpatialShell` `left`/`right`/`bottom` slots are now declarative (design §2.6's own recorded deviation -- `SpatialShell` still owns the drag, it only asks which content sits on which edge): a small `Map<ActionId, @Composable () -> Unit>` keyed by `fylz.room.locations`/`.tools`/`.recovery` is looked up through `registry.edgeRooms()[EDGE_LEFT/RIGHT/BOTTOM]`, replacing the hard-coded assignment; same three rooms land on the same three edges as before, since that's exactly what `BuiltInActions.roomOpenGestures()` already binds. `commandPaletteOpen`'s dialog call site and the new `showRegistryProblemsDialog` flag/dialog were added alongside the existing dialogs. Nothing removed from `FylzV1App.kt` this commit -- it's the one commit in the six-commit plan that adds real logic (key routing, gesture dispatch, focus management) rather than moving UI out, so it grows instead of shrinking.  **MC.0f** (design §2.8 item 6, the closing commit): moved `enum class WorkspacePane { PRIMARY, SECONDARY }` verbatim from `workspace/DesktopWorkspacePolicy.kt` into `workspace/DualPaneModels.kt` (same package, unchanged; `DualPaneModels.kt` already used it and stays the only definition, per M12.1's own "the `workspace/` models exist"); grep-verified zero remaining references, then deleted `workspace/KeyboardShortcutPolicy.kt`, `workspace/DesktopWorkspacePolicy.kt` and `app/src/test/.../workspace/KeyboardShortcutPolicyTest.kt` -- both tables were dead (design §1: reachable from nothing, no `onKeyEvent` anywhere before MC.0e), so the registry (`actions/BuiltInActions.kt` + `actions/KeyRouter.kt`, landed MC.0a/e) is now the only source of shortcuts. Froze the differential oracle: `actions/BuiltInActions.kt`'s `enabledWhen` lambdas for `fylz.rename`/`fylz.extract`/`fylz.pdf.tools`/`fylz.paste`/`fylz.new-folder`/`fylz.new-file`/`fylz.scan-to-pdf`/`fylz.find-duplicates`/`fylz.ai.organize`/`fylz.select.all`/`fylz.favourite.toggle` now inline the same expressions `LegacyAvailability` held, word for word (`EntryKind`/`isZipFamilyArchive` imported directly); `ui/FylzV1App.kt`'s one remaining call (`BrowserState.canNavigateUp`) inlined the same way (`(activeTab?.locations?.size ?: 0) > 1`), dropping the `LegacyAvailability` import.
