`cargo fmt --check` clean; `cargo deny check` green (same pre-existing `syn` duplicate-version/license-not-encountered warnings as every prior MC.0 commit, nothing new). | See `docs/agent/DESIGN-MC0-ACTION-REGISTRY.md` for the full design; this row is MC.0a of its §2.8 six-commit plan (types, `BrowserState`, registry/resolver/dispatcher, the legacy oracle). Two resolved ambiguities, both chosen to preserve current behaviour and keep `registry.problems` empty for the built-ins (the acceptance bar in design §2.4/§7): (1) design §2.4's parenthetical list of "targetless" placements names `Shortcut` and `Room` alongside `CommandPalette`/`Toolbar`/`Menu`/`SelectionBar`, but §2.5's own table places `requiresTarget` built-ins (`fylz.open` on `Shortcut(Enter)`; `fylz.tab.close` on `Room(LOCATIONS)` and `Shortcut(Ctrl+W)`) on exactly those two — each with a documented, conventional way to obtain its target (the focused entry, the active tab, the row it's drawn against). `TargetRequiredOnTargetlessPlacement` is implemented over `{CommandPalette, Toolbar, Menu, SelectionBar}` only. (2) `fylz.customisation.problems`'s `visibleWhen` should read `registry.problems.isNotEmpty()`, but a `(BrowserState) -> Boolean` predicate cannot see the registry it is itself part of; since the built-in table's own `problems` is empty by construction (asserted by `ShortcutTableTest`), `visibleWhen = { false }` is exactly "never for the shipped built-ins" (design §4) today — revisited once a condition can reach outside `BrowserState` (MC.1+). Also: `fylz.recycle-bin` is one `BuiltInBinding` carrying two `Room` placements (`LIBRARY_RAIL` and `TOOLS`), not two bindings, since `ActionId` is unique per action — an early draft that duplicated it tripped both `ActionIdGrammarTest`'s uniqueness check and `ActionRegistry`'s own `DuplicateId` problem, caught before committing. Nine built-ins (`fylz.commands`, `fylz.search.focus`, `fylz.customisation.problems`, `fylz.history.files`/`.backup.plans`/`.backup.import`/`.archive.tools`, `fylz.protect`, `fylz.archive.inspect`) have a documented no-op `run` in MC.0a: their current UI (the command palette, search-field focus, the customisation-problems row, the self-contained Recovery/Archive-Tools overlay composables) has no externally callable "open" hook to delegate to yet, and inventing one would be new behaviour, not a refactor — each is wired for real once its surface exists (MC.0d/e). Not done in MC.0a, per its own scope: no renderer reads the registry (MC.0b–e); `KeyRouter`/gesture dispatch/the command palette (MC.0e); `NoHardCodedMenusTest`/`FylzV1AppSizeTest` (MC.0d/MC.0f, not expected to pass yet).  **MC.0b resolved ambiguities/decisions** (both chosen to preserve behaviour, per the design's own instruction): (1) the design's bullet says the renderer takes "(registry, state, dispatcher) (or the resolver)" -- implemented as `(resolver: ActionResolver, dispatcher: ActionDispatcher, state: BrowserState, ctx: ActionContext)`, since every renderer needs both `ActionResolver.resolve` and `ActionDispatcher.run`, and the resolver already wraps the registry. (2) `BuiltinIcons.kt` is scoped to the icon names the selection bar's built-ins actually use (12 entries) rather than the full 57-built-in table: several of the table's other icon names (e.g. the Tools room's `"Brightness"`) don't obviously correspond to a real `Icons.Outlined.*` symbol and belong to a surface (Rooms) this commit doesn't render -- MC.0d/e extend the same object as their own renderers need it, per the design's own per-commit split. Behaviour preserved exactly: same 12 items, same order, same enabled states, same labels, same icons, same handlers on click; `ActionResolverGoldenTest` green unchanged.  **MC.0c resolved ambiguities/decisions:** (1) the command palette is moved up from MC.0e into this commit per the task's own instruction, since `fylz.commands` first becomes a real, clickable overflow item here -- shipping it as a dead menu entry for two more commits would be worse than building the (already-designed, §2.6) palette slightly early; `paletteVisible` was added to `BuiltInBinding` now rather than in MC.0e for the same reason. (2) `BrowserRowRenderer` renders `Toolbar(BROWSER_ROW)`'s two items (`fylz.navigate.up`, `fylz.select.all`) as two separate composables placed at their existing visual positions around the unchanged search field and sort menu, rather than as one contiguous resolved list -- the search field and its scope chips are not actions and design left where to host them as this commit's own call; keeping today's exact left-to-right order (navigate-up, search field, sort, select-all) was chosen over a generic "render the whole placement as a run" renderer, since the two aren't adjacent in the real layout. (3) The overflow menu's leading icons are reproduced exactly as before: only `fylz.new-folder`/`fylz.new-file`/`fylz.scan-to-pdf` get one (matching the old code's own `DropdownMenuItem`s verbatim); `fylz.find-duplicates`/`fylz.ai.organize` stay unadorned, and the new `fylz.commands` item was left unadorned too rather than inventing an icon for it, matching its unadorned neighbours since there was no old code to preserve for a brand-new item. (4) `BuiltinIcons.kt` gained exactly the 7 names this commit's renderers look up (not the full remaining built-in table) -- same per-commit scoping as MC.0b's note; Rooms/Recovery/Archive-Tools/gesture icons still wait for MC.0d's renderer. Behaviour identical except design's own deviation (b): the new "Commands" overflow item and the palette it opens. `ActionResolverGoldenTest` green unchanged.  **MC.0d resolved ambiguities/decisions:** (1) `RegistryProblem`'s own KDoc (written in MC.0a) already resolves the design §2.4 vs §2.5 tension over which placements `TargetRequiredOnTargetlessPlacement` covers -- `Shortcut`/`Room` are excluded because the built-in table gives their `requiresTarget` actions a documented conventional target (Enter -> focused, Ctrl+W -> active tab, `fylz.tab.close`'s `Room(LOCATIONS)` -> the row's own tab); this commit is the first to actually exercise that Locations-room convention, dispatching `fylz.tab.close` with `ActionTarget.Tab(tab.id)` from the row's own trailing close icon, and it checks out against `ShortcutTableTest`'s existing "problems is empty for the built-ins" assertion. (2) The Recovery room's card title stays a fixed string ("Operation history") for `fylz.history.operations` rather than the resolved, count-carrying `item.label` ("Operation history (N)") -- the design says the *description* varies with `operationsNeedingAttention`, and the old code's own title never did; the resolved label is still what the command palette and any future shortcut-hint surface would show, so nothing is lost, only the card's own title stays literal per "today's exact icon/title/description text". (3) `ArchiveToolsMenuDialog` takes `resolver`/`state` and an `onSelect: (ActionId) -> Unit)` the overlay maps to its two existing lambdas, rather than dispatching through `ActionDispatcher` -- both `fylz.protect`/`fylz.archive.inspect` `run` bodies are, and remain, documented no-ops (the overlay's own picker launchers are the actual behaviour), so routing a click through `dispatcher.run` would silently do nothing; the renderer stays a pure resolved-list-in, id-out view exactly as this task's brief suggested. (4) `FylzV1App`/`FylzV1Workspace` lost the `recoveryRoom` composable parameter entirely rather than keeping it unused -- once the Recovery room is built inline from the registry (the same way Locations/Tools already are, both reading `actionResolver`/`actionDispatcher`/`browserState`/`actionContext` that only exist inside `FylzV1Workspace`), `FylzAppShell` has nothing left to hand down through that slot; `onShowHistory`/`operationsNeedingAttention` (already threaded in MC.0a) are the only pieces `FylzAppShell` still owns that the registry needs, and both stay exactly as they were. Nothing left unfinished for MC.0d; `KeyRouter`, gesture dispatch, `edgeRooms()` wiring and the Problems row are MC.0e's.  **MC.0e resolved ambiguities/decisions:** (1) The design's own text says `route(...): Routed?` and separately describes Escape routing "to a `ClearFocus` sentinel (not an action)" -- a single return type can't be an `ActionId`-carrying thing in one branch and something that isn't in another unless both live under one sealed type, so `Routed` is that type: `Routed.Dispatch(id, target)` (the design's literal `Routed(id, target)`) and `Routed.ClearFocus` as peer variants. (2) The workspace root's re-focus rule ("whenever a text field or dialog releases it") is implemented as one `LaunchedEffect(windowFocused, textFieldFocused)` reading `LocalWindowInfo.current.isWindowFocused`, rather than hooking every individual dialog's own `onDismiss` -- every dialog in this file (`AlertDialog`, `androidx.compose.ui.window.Dialog`, the Recovery overlays' own) opens a *separate Android window*, so the Activity window loses/regains window focus around each one regardless of which dialog it is; one generic check covers all of them (including ones this commit never touched) at once and self-corrects if a future dialog is added without updating a hand-maintained list. (3) `CommandPaletteDialog` gained `onFocusChanged` and reports its own field's focus into the same shared flag the search field uses, per the design's literal text -- in practice this is moot, since the palette is itself a separate-window `Dialog` and the workspace root's `onKeyEvent` never sees a key while any such window is focused; wiring it anyway keeps this dialog's own behaviour honest with what the design describes, and costs nothing. (4) The row/card gesture refactor changed `FileRowV1`/`FileCard`/`SearchResults`'s signatures (dropping `onOpen`/`onOpenExternal`/`onToggleSelection` for `dispatcher`/`state`/`ctx`, which `FileBrowser` already carried) rather than layering `dispatcher.gesture` calls inside the existing three callbacks -- `FileBrowser` itself keeps its own signature apart from the one new `onSearchFocusChanged` param the brief asked to thread, so this stays within "do not restructure FileBrowser": only its call sites into the three row/card composables changed, not its own shape or the search field's position in the layout. (5) `fylz.customisation.problems`'s `run` needed a real hook once its `visibleWhen` could turn true; `ActionContext.showRegistryProblems()` was added rather than routing through the four self-contained Recovery overlays' pattern, since this row has an imperative "open a dialog" exactly like `fylz.history.operations`' FAB does, not a self-contained composable with its own state. Nothing left unfinished for MC.0e; MC.0f retires the two dead shortcut policies, ratchets the size test, and freezes the golden table.  **MC.0f resolved ambiguities/decisions:** (1) `KeyboardShortcutPolicyTest.kt` turned out to hold exactly one `@Test` method (four assertions inside it, not four separate cases as an earlier note in this row estimated) -- corrected here rather than left wrong, since the Tests column's own arithmetic (424 -> 425) depends on it. (2) The design's instruction to lift `LegacyAvailability`'s functions into the golden test "with a comment naming the `FylzV1App.kt` line it was lifted from at `6e3ab3f`" was read as the *pre-refactor* line, not today's -- each `LegacyOracle` function's doc comment cites the exact line(s) re-verified against `git show 6e3ab3f:.../FylzV1App.kt` (`:1133`, `:1134-1136`, `:1137-1138`, `:1041`, `:1076`, `:1082`, `:1088`, `:1099`, `:1121`, `:1752`, `:1667`), matching design §1's own citations for the same expressions. `canNavigateUp` was not copied into `LegacyOracle`, since nothing in `ActionResolverGoldenTest` ever called `LegacyAvailability.canNavigateUp` -- it asserts against `state.canNavigateUp` directly (`BrowserState`'s own field), so freezing it would be dead code the design didn't ask for. (3) Two of `BuiltInActions.kt`'s inlined predicates (`canPdfTools`, `findDuplicatesEnabled`) needed a named lambda parameter (`{ state -> ... }`) rather than the implicit `it`, since the original expression itself iterates a nested `it` (`selection.all { it.kind == ... }`) that would otherwise shadow the outer `BrowserState` parameter -- same logic, only the parameter name changed to avoid a silent shadow. (4) `WorkspacePane` was inserted at the top of `DualPaneModels.kt` (before `PaneLayout`), the file's own first type, since nothing in the design specifies where within the file it should land and the enum other types in the file already depend on reads naturally first. Nothing left unfinished: MC.0's six-commit plan (design §2.8) is complete, the gate is green, and every §7 acceptance line holds. |

## Master plan

`docs/agent/MASTER_PLAN.md` was added this run (24 Sep 2026). Per its own §0 and §8, it supersedes
`FYLZ_CLAUDE_CODE_INSTRUCTIONS.md` for everything after P1.7 and its own non-negotiables/workflow
are restated there so it stands alone. Its own "first instructions for this run" say: commit the
file, read this progress log, and continue with **M1 / P1.8** -- exactly where this run already
was. M1 restates the remaining Phase 1 tasks (P1.8-P1.14) under the same IDs, so this progress log
continues to use them unchanged; M1's own gate, **GATE-M1**, needs Madhav to run the device checks,
and this run stops there (M2.1-M2.3 -- the Rust core workspace/FFI-skeleton/Gradle bootstrap -- are
explicitly allowed to start before that gate closes, since they "don't touch app behaviour"; M3
onward waits for it).

**Addendum 1** (`docs/agent/MASTER_PLAN_ADDENDUM_1.md`, 25 Sep 2026) amends the above and wins where
the two conflict. Its own section A replaces the master plan's single-gate-stops-everything policy
(section 0.3) with two kinds of gate: **review gates** (GATE-M1, GATE-M2, GATE-M4, GATE-M8's
technical parts) now log-and-continue -- write the milestone report, add device checks, append an
entry to the new `docs/agent/REVIEW_QUEUE.md`, and keep going, rather than stopping the run; **hard
gates** (GATE-A, GATE-M8's model choice, GATE-M10, GATE-D-cloud, GATE-UT, D5, GATE-C1, GATE-C2)
still never pass without Madhav -- build up to the point of no return, record it in
`REVIEW_QUEUE.md`, and continue with other independent work rather than publishing, submitting or
enabling anything. It also adds a large customisation program (sections B-E: three-tier
customisation, an Action registry, Steps/rules/scripts/bundles) and a new milestone **MC**, with
**MC.0** (the action registry) inserted to run **before M3.2** and MC.1-MC.9 after M9/before M10.
Section A's own "Housekeeping before continuing" list -- committing the uncommitted M3.1 work and
logging it, writing `REPORT-M1.md`/`REPORT-M2.md`, and renaming/updating PR #19 -- is what the
commits immediately following this one carry out.

## Deviations

- Branch name substitution (above), forced by the harness's fixed per-repo branch assignment.
- JDK 17 → 21 substitution (above); re-verify the "frozen toolchain" claim in section 1.5 stays
  otherwise intact if this matters to a future run.
- **M3.1 work (vendoring `libarchive`, a `fylz-archive` read path) was found already written but
  uncommitted in this working tree by a later run/session, not this one** -- both this row's own
  GATE-M1 note and M2.6's GATE-M2 note above say M3 waits on Madhav, and there is no record here of
  that approval having happened. Rather than leave real, working, uncommitted code exposed to loss
  (container reclamation), it was verified as-is (`cargo build/test/clippy -D warnings/deny check`
  all green for the host target; `cargo fmt --check` failures were fixed, a formatting-only change)
  and committed **to this session's local history only, not yet pushed**, pending Madhav confirming
  the M1/M2 gates were actually cleared to start M3. Not independently verified at that point: the
  Android cross-compile path (`cargo ndk`) -- only the host build was exercised then.
  **Resolved by Addendum 1 §A.1:** the gate policy changed (GATE-M1/GATE-M2 are now review gates --
  logged in `docs/agent/REVIEW_QUEUE.md` and continued past, never a stop) and §A's own housekeeping
  list explicitly directs this commit to be made and logged as M3.1 -- see the M3.1 row above for
  what shipped and what's still outstanding within it. The commit making this edit also pushes the
  branch for the first time since M2.6, so `5490b0d` and everything after it reaches `origin`.
